\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}
, we should show that

a[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]=a[\overrightarrow{\gamma}][\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]
. Again, when

T=B
it reduces to a property for neutral terms and
T=\square T^{\prime}
we can apply
IH immediately. When
T=S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}
, then both sides become

\displaystyle(f,\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Gamma},a:\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}})\mapsto f(\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}\circ(\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}),a)

and

\displaystyle(f,\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Gamma},a:\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}})\mapsto f((\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime})\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma},a)

which just reduce to associativity of composition of unified weakenings.
∎

Lemma 5.7.

\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
is a functor.

Proof.

The functorial laws follow immediately from those of
\hat{\top}
,
\hat{\times}
and

\llbracket T\rrbracket
.
∎

Lemma 5.8.

\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket
is a functor.

Proof.

We only show the case where
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}=\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma
. First we consider the
identity law. We should show that given
\overrightarrow{\rho}:\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
,

\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}}]
is still
\overrightarrow{\rho}
. Expanding the definition, it
means the following function should be identity

\displaystyle((n,\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}:\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{n}}),\rho:\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}})\mapsto((\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}}},{n}),\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}}\mid{n}]),\rho[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}}])

Since
\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}}},{n})=n
and
\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}}\mid{n}=\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}}
, we have

\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}}\mid{n}]=\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}
by IH.
\rho=\rho[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}}]
by the
functoriality of
\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
.

We then show the composition law. Given
\overrightarrow{\gamma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Delta}
and

\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}
, we should show

\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}][\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]=\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]
. Expanding the definitions and let
((n,\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}),\rho):=\overrightarrow{\rho}
, we have

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}][\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]=((\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}},{n})}),\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{n}][\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}},{n})}]),\rho[\overrightarrow{\gamma}][\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}])

and

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]=((\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}},{n}),\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}[(\overrightarrow{\gamma}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime})\mid{n}]),\rho[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}])

We know that
\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}},{n})=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}},{n})})
and

(\overrightarrow{\gamma}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime})\mid{n}=(\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{n})\circ(\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}},{n})})
are
basic properties for unified weakenings. These allow us to prove

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{n}][\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}},{n})}]=\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}[(\overrightarrow{\gamma}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime})\mid{n}]

by IH.

Lastly,
\rho[\overrightarrow{\gamma}][\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]=\rho[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]
holds by
functoriality of
\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
.
∎

5.2.3.
Normalization

Next, we shall interpret the terms as natural transformation. Just like the syntactic
case, we need to define
L
and truncation for the interpretation of context
stacks:

(Truncation Offset)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}({\_},{\_})

\displaystyle:\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}\to\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{0})

\displaystyle:=0

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}({((n,\overrightarrow{\rho}),\rho)},{1+m})

\displaystyle:=n+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{m})

(Truncation)

\displaystyle\_\mid{\_}

\displaystyle:(\overrightarrow{\rho}:\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}})\;(n:\mathbb{N})\to\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\mid{n}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{0}

\displaystyle:=\overrightarrow{\rho}

\displaystyle((n,\overrightarrow{\rho}),\rho)\mid{1+m}

\displaystyle:=\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{m}

In these two functions, we implicitly assume the truncation size,
n
, is strictly smaller
than
|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}|
for the truncation to make sense. With these two functions defined, we
can define the interpretation of terms as natural transformations:

\displaystyle\llbracket\_\rrbracket

\displaystyle:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T\to\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket\Rightarrow\llbracket T\rrbracket

(expanded form)

\displaystyle\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

\displaystyle:\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}\to\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

(where
\rho(x)
performs projections properly for lookup)

\displaystyle\llbracket x\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}((\_,\rho))

\displaystyle:=\rho(x)

(
*
is
the

element of the chosen singleton set)

\displaystyle\llbracket\texttt{box}\ t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle:=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}((1,\overrightarrow{\rho}),*)

(where
m:=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})
and
\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{m}\!\!{}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{m};\cdot
)

\displaystyle\llbracket\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle:=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{m}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n})[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{m}\!\!{}]

(where
(\pi,\rho):=\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]
)

\displaystyle\llbracket\lambda x:S.t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle:=(\overrightarrow{\gamma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Delta})(a:\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}})\mapsto\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}((\pi,(\rho,a)))

\displaystyle\llbracket t\ s\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle:=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho},\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}~,~\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}))

We need a reification function to read from values in natural transformations back to
syntactic terms. This is done by defining two natural transformations: reflect goes
from neutral terms to natural transformations, and reify goes from natural transformations to normal
terms.

\displaystyle\downarrow^{T}

\displaystyle:\llbracket T\rrbracket\Rightarrow\textsf{Nf}\ T

\displaystyle\downarrow^{B}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(a)

\displaystyle:=a

\displaystyle\downarrow^{\square T}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(a:\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot})

\displaystyle:=\texttt{box}\ \downarrow^{T}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot}(a)

(where
p(\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}}):\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma,x{:}S\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma
)

\displaystyle\downarrow^{S\longrightarrow T}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma}(a)

\displaystyle:=\lambda x.\downarrow^{T}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:S)}(a~(p(\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}})~,~{\uparrow^{S}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:S)}\!(x)}))

\displaystyle\uparrow^{T}

\displaystyle:\textsf{Ne}\ T\Rightarrow\llbracket T\rrbracket

\displaystyle\uparrow^{B}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(v)

\displaystyle:=v

\displaystyle\uparrow^{\square T}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(v)

\displaystyle:=\uparrow^{T}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot}({\texttt{unbox}_{1}\ v})

\displaystyle\uparrow^{S\longrightarrow T}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma}(v)

\displaystyle:=(\overrightarrow{\gamma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma)(a)\mapsto\uparrow^{T}_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(v[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\ \downarrow^{S}_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(a))

Finally, we define the identity environment
\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
as

\displaystyle\uparrow

\displaystyle:(\overrightarrow{\Gamma}:\overrightarrow{\textsf{Ctx}})\to\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle\uparrow^{\epsilon;\cdot}

\displaystyle:=(*,*)

\displaystyle\uparrow^{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot}

\displaystyle:=((1,\uparrow^{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}),*)

((where
(\pi,\rho):=\uparrow^{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma}[p(\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}})]
))

\displaystyle\uparrow^{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma,x:T}

\displaystyle:=(\pi,(\rho,\uparrow^{T}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma,x:T}\!\!(x)))\hfill

This allows us to finally write down the normalization algorithm:

Definition 5.9.

A normalization by evaluation algorithm given
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T
is

\displaystyle\textsf{nbe}^{T}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(t)

\displaystyle:=\downarrow^{T}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(\uparrow^{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}))

5.3.
Properties of Interpretations

In this section we consider properties used for correctness proofs below.

5.3.1.
Basic Properties

Lemmas in this section are semantic counterparts of those in
Section

3.6
.
Let
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
and
\overrightarrow{\gamma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Delta}
:

Lemma 5.10 (Distributivity of Addition over Truncation Offset).

If
n+m<|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}|
, then
\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{(n+m)})=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n}},{m})
.

Proof.

This lemma is essentially the semantic counterpart for
Lemma

3.8
. The proof
is also essentially the same.
∎

Lemma 5.11 (Monotonicity of Truncation Offset).

If
n<|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}|
, then
\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]},{n})=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})})
.

Proof.

This lemma is essentially the semantic counterpart for
Lemma

3.9
. We shall
proceed the proof in a similar way:

Case

n=0
, immediate.

Case

n=1+n^{\prime}
, then
((k,\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}),\rho):=\overrightarrow{\rho}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}({((k,\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}),\rho)[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]},{1+n^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({((\mathcal{O}({\gamma},{k}),\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{k}]),\rho[\overrightarrow{\gamma}])},{(1+n^{\prime})})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\gamma},{k})+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{k}]},{n^{\prime}})

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\gamma},{k})+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{k}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}},{n^{\prime}})})

(by
Lemma

3.8
)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}},{k+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}},{n^{\prime}})})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}},{(1+n^{\prime})})})

∎

Lemma 5.12 (Monotonicity of Truncation).

If
n<|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}|
, then
\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\mid{n}=(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n})[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}]
.

Proof.

This lemma is essentially the semantic counterpart for
Lemma

3.11
. We shall
proceed the proof in a similar way:

Case

n=0
, immediate.

Case

n=1+n^{\prime}
, then
((k,\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}),\rho):=\overrightarrow{\rho}
and

\displaystyle(((k,\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}),\rho)[\overrightarrow{\gamma}])\mid{(1+n^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=((\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}},{k}),\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{k}]),\rho[\overrightarrow{\gamma}])\mid{(1+n^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{k}]\mid{n^{\prime}}

(by IH)

\displaystyle=(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}\mid{n^{\prime}})[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{k}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}},{n^{\prime}})}]

(by
Lemma

3.10
)

\displaystyle=(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}\mid{n^{\prime}})[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{(k+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}},{n^{\prime}}))}]

\displaystyle=(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{(1+n^{\prime})})[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{1+n^{\prime}})}]

∎

5.3.2.
Naturality of
\llbracket t\rrbracket

As we claimed, this interpretation is indeed a natural transformation:

Lemma 5.13.

Given
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T
,
\llbracket t\rrbracket
is a natural transformation from

\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket
to
\llbracket T\rrbracket
:

•

Given
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
,
\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})\in\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
.

•

Given
\overrightarrow{\gamma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}
and

\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}
,

\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}])
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\_[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\_[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]}

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T
.

Case

t=x
, then it works immediately by analysis.

Case

t=\texttt{box}\ t^{\prime}
, then

\displaystyle\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\cdot}((1,\overrightarrow{\rho}),*)[\overrightarrow{\gamma};\Uparrow^{1}\!\!{}]

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}(((1,\overrightarrow{\rho}),*)[\overrightarrow{\gamma};\Uparrow^{1}\!\!{}])

(by monotonicity of
\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\rrbracket
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}((1,\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]),*)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}])

Case

t=\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ t^{\prime}
, then the left hand side has

\displaystyle\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n})[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}\!\!{}][\overrightarrow{\gamma}]

(by functoriality)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n})[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}\!\!{}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}]

and the right hand side has

\displaystyle\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}])

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\mid{n})[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}\!\!{}]

(where
m:=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})})=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]},{n})
by

Lemma

5.11
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{m}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime})[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{m}\!\!{}]

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n})[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}][\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{m}\!\!{}]

(by functoriality)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n})[(\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})};\Uparrow^{1}\!\!{})\circ(\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{m}\!\!{})]

Comparing both sides, we realize that we only need to prove

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}\!\!{}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}=(\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{(\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n}))};\Uparrow^{1}\!\!{})\circ(\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{m}\!\!{})

but they both evaluate to
\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})};\Uparrow^{m}\!\!{}
. Diagrammatically,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{m};\cdot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})};\cdot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{\gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{m}\!\!{}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}\!\!{}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})};\Uparrow^{1}\!\!{}}

Case

t=\lambda x.t^{\prime}
and
T=S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}
, we should verify
\llbracket t\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})\in\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
. We assume
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
, and further
assume
\overrightarrow{\gamma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
and
a:\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
because

\llbracket T\rrbracket
is a presheaf exponential. Then we should check that the naturality
condition of the presheaf exponential holds. This is given by further assuming

\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Delta}
, and our goal becomes

\displaystyle\llbracket\lambda x.t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})(\overrightarrow{\gamma}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime},a[\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}])=\llbracket\lambda x.t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})(\overrightarrow{\gamma},a)[\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]

We evaluate both sides:

(where
(\pi,\rho):=\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]
)

\displaystyle\llbracket\lambda x.t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})(\overrightarrow{\gamma}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime},a[\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}])

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}((\pi,(\rho,a[\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}])))

(where
(\pi^{\prime},\rho^{\prime}):=\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]
)

\displaystyle\llbracket\lambda x.t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})(\overrightarrow{\gamma},a)[\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}((\pi^{\prime},(\rho^{\prime},a)))[\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}((\pi^{\prime},(\rho^{\prime},a))[\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}])

Notice that by the definition of monotonicity of context stacks,
(\pi,(\rho,a[\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]))=(\pi^{\prime},(\rho^{\prime},a))[\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]
and thus the interpretation is indeed
well-defined.

Case

t=t^{\prime}\ s
, immediate from the fact that
\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket
gives a presheaf
exponential and thus this case is immediately
discharged.

∎

5.4.
Relation with MoT

As previously discussed, syntactic MoTs can be uniformly represented as
unified substitutions (even more specifically, unified weakening). In the presheaf
model, MoTs can be modeled by operations manipulating the evaluation environments. In order to state
the relation, we need the following operation, which intuitively shrinks the
interpretation of a context stack based on a syntactic MoT
\{k/l\}
:

Definition 5.14.

The
shrink
operation rearranges
\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
to

\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
according to
\{k/l\}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}
. More concretely,
if
\{k/l\}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}
where

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}=\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma_{0};\cdots;(\Gamma_{k},\Delta_{0});\cdots;\Delta_{l}
and

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}=\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma_{0};\Delta_{0};\cdots;\Delta_{l}
, then
shrink

converts
\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
to
\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
:

\displaystyle\textsf{shrink}(\_,k,l)

\displaystyle:\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}\to\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

(where
\rho_{1}\in\llbracket\Gamma_{0}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
and
\rho_{2}\in\llbracket\Delta_{0}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
are extracted from
\rho
)

\displaystyle\textsf{shrink}((\pi,\rho\in\llbracket\Gamma_{0},\Delta_{0}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}),0,0)

\displaystyle:=((0,(\pi,\rho_{1})),\rho_{2})

(where
\rho_{2}
is extracted from
\rho
per above)

\displaystyle\textsf{shrink}((\pi,\rho),1+k^{\prime},0)

\displaystyle:=((\mathcal{O}({\pi,\rho},{(1+k^{\prime})}),(\pi,\rho)\mid{(1+k^{\prime})}),\rho_{2})

\displaystyle\textsf{shrink}(((n,\overrightarrow{\rho}),\rho),k,1+l^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=((n,\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,l^{\prime})),\rho)

We can check that this function is well-defined and it decreases by
l
. Next we shall
investigate its interaction with truncation and truncation offset. We consistently use
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}

and
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}
in the next few lemmas as in the definition above.

Lemma 5.15.

Given
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
and
n\leq l
,

\mathcal{O}({\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,l)},{n})=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})
.

Lemma 5.16.

Given
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
and
n\leq l
,

\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,l)\mid{n}=\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n},k,l-n)
.

Proof of
Lemmas

5.15
and
5.16
.

Induction on
n
. By inverting
n\leq l
, we know we always in the last case of

shrink
, except when
n=0
, where the equations hold automatically.
∎

Lemma 5.17.

Given
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
and
n>l
,

\mathcal{O}({\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,l)},{n})=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{(k+n-1)})
.

Proof.

Based on
Lemma

5.15
, it is sufficient to consider the case where
l=0
, and thus
n>0
and
n=1+m
for some
m
.

Let
(\pi,\rho):=\overrightarrow{\rho}
. We consider the case whether
k=0
:

Case

k=0
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}({\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},0,0)},{n})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({((0,(\pi,\rho_{1})),\rho_{2})},{1+m})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({(\pi,\rho_{1})},{m})

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n-1})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({(\pi,\rho)},{m})

We know that for both sides, only
\pi
impacts the value and thus they are equal.

Case

k\neq 0
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}({\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,0)},{n})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({((\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{k}),\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{k}),\rho_{2})},{1+m})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{k})+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{k}},{m})

(
Lemma

5.10
)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{k+m})

∎

However, if we think
\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,l)\mid{n}=\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{(k+n-1)}
given the
preconditions of the previous lemma, then we are wrong. For a counterexample, if

\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma_{0},\Delta_{0})\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
, then

\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},0,0)\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma_{0};\Delta_{0}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
. Moreover, if
we take
n=1
, then
\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},0,0)\mid{1}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma_{0}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
, while
\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{0}=\overrightarrow{\rho}
so they are not even in the same set and cannot be compared!
Fortunately, this is the only group of counterexamples, and we can refine our equation a
bit further.

Lemma 5.18.

Given
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
,
n>l
and
k\neq 0
,

\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,l)\mid{n}=\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{(k+n-1)}
.

Proof.

If we know
k\neq 0
, then we can compare both sides as they can be verified in the
same set. The proof goes similarly by induction on
l
and compute accordingly.
∎

If we truncate a bit more, then we can say something when
k=0
:

Lemma 5.19.

Given
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
,
n>l+1
,

\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},0,l)\mid{n}=\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{(n-1)}
.

Proof.

By truncating one more, we avoided the counterexample. The proof again goes very
similarly.
∎

We show that
shrink
and monotonicity commute:

Lemma 5.20.

Given
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
and
\overrightarrow{\gamma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Delta}
,

\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}],k,l)=\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,l)[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
l
and consider the case of
k
. The proof is just
straightforward calculation.

Case

l=0
,
k=0
, let
(\pi,\rho):=\overrightarrow{\rho}
,

\displaystyle\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}],0,0)

\displaystyle=((0,(\pi,\rho_{1})[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]),\rho_{2}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}])

\displaystyle=((0,(\pi,\rho_{1}),\rho_{2}))[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]

\displaystyle=\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},0,0)[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]

Case

l=0
,
k\neq 0
, let
(\pi,\rho):=\overrightarrow{\rho}
,

\displaystyle\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}],k,0)

\displaystyle=((\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]},{k}),\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\mid{k}),\rho_{2}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}])

(by
Lemmas

5.11
and
5.12
)

\displaystyle=((\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{k})}),\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{k}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{k})}]),\rho_{2}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}])

\displaystyle=((\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{k}),\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{k}),\rho_{2})[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]

\displaystyle=\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,0)[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]

Case

l=1+l^{\prime}
, let
((n,\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}),\rho):=\overrightarrow{\rho}
,

\displaystyle\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}],k,l)

\displaystyle=\textsf{shrink}(((\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}},{n}),\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{n}]),\rho[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]),k,1+l^{\prime})

\displaystyle=((\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}},{n}),\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{n}],k,l^{\prime})),\rho[\overrightarrow{\gamma}])

(by IH)

\displaystyle=((\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}},{n}),\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime},k,l^{\prime})[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{n}]),\rho[\overrightarrow{\gamma}])

\displaystyle=((n,\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime},k,l^{\prime})),\rho)[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]

\displaystyle=\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,1+l^{\prime})[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]

∎

Then we shall state the relation between MoT and
\llbracket t\rrbracket
:

Theorem 5.21.

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\vdash t:T
, then given

\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
,
\llbracket t\{k/l\}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,l))
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
t
.

Case

t=x
, immediate by analyzing all cases of
shrink
.

Case

t=\texttt{box}\ t^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\llbracket t\{k/l\}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\{k/l+1\}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}((1,\overrightarrow{\rho}),\cdot)

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}(\textsf{shrink}(((1,\overrightarrow{\rho}),\cdot),k,l+1))

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}((1,\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,l+1)),\cdot)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,l))

Case

t=\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ t^{\prime}
,

Subcase

n\leq l
, let
n^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}({\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,l)},{n})
and
\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}=\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,l)
.

\displaystyle\llbracket t\{k/l\}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\{k/l-n\}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}|_{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n})[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}\!\!{}]

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n},k,l-n))[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}\!\!{}]

(by
Lemmas

5.15
and
5.16
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{n^{\prime}}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}\mid{n})[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{n^{\prime}}\!\!{}]

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,l))

Subcase

n>l
, let
n^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}({\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,l)},{n})
and
\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}=\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,l)
.

\displaystyle\llbracket t\{k/l\}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{(\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{k+n-1}))}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{(k+n-1)})[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{k+n-1})}\!\!{}]

Notice that there is an implicit syntactic weakening here for
t^{\prime}
for the
interpretation to make sense.

\displaystyle\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,l))=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{n^{\prime}}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}\mid{n})[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{n^{\prime}}\!\!{}]

By
Lemma

5.17
, we can show
n^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{k+n-1})
but as
previously discussed, we cannot have
\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{(k+n-1)}=\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}\mid{n}
in
general, so we have not done here. In fact, counterexamples for this equation
only exist when
k=0
and
n=l+1
.

Nevertheless, we observe that

\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{n^{\prime}}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{(k+n-1)})=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{n^{\prime}}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}\mid{n})
holds. Notice that
\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{(k+n-1)}=\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{l}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma_{0},\Delta_{0})\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{n^{\prime}}}
only
has more values than
\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}\mid{n}=\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}\mid{(l+1)}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma_{0}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{n^{\prime}}}
in the topmost context.

\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{n^{\prime}}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{(k+n-1)})=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{n^{\prime}}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}\mid{n})
can be seen as weakening as
t^{\prime}
does not
refer to the extra values in
\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n}
.

Case

t=\lambda x:S.t^{\prime}
, then

(where
(\pi,\rho):=\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]
)

\displaystyle\llbracket t\{k/l\}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle=(\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime},\overrightarrow{\gamma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Delta},a:\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}})\mapsto\llbracket t^{\prime}\{k/l\}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}((\pi,(\rho,a)))

\displaystyle=(\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime},\overrightarrow{\gamma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Delta},a:\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}})\mapsto\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}(\textsf{shrink}((\pi,(\rho,a)),k,l))

(by
Lemma

5.20
, where
(\pi^{\prime},\rho^{\prime}):=\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,l)[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]
)

\displaystyle=(\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime},\overrightarrow{\gamma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Delta},a:\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}})\mapsto\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}(\pi^{\prime},(\rho^{\prime},a))

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},k,l))

Case

t=t^{\prime}\ s
, immediate by IH.

∎

Corollary 5.22.

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\mid{n};\cdot\vdash t:T
, then given

\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
,

\llbracket t\{n/0\}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}((\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n}),\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n}),*)
.

Proof.

Specialize the previous theorem. Also notice

\textsf{shrink}(\overrightarrow{\rho},n,0)=((\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n}),\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n}),*)
.
∎

5.5.
Relation with Term Substitutions

Next we consider the relation between term substitution and evaluation
environments. This relation is much more classical than the previous one.

Definition 5.23.

The
insert
operation inserts a semantic value into an interpretation of context
stacks. More formally, given
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime});\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
where
|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}|=n
, and
a\in\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}
, then
\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho},n,a)\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:T,\Gamma^{\prime});\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
.

(where
\rho^{\prime}
is
\rho
with
a
inserted in the proper spot)

\displaystyle\textsf{insert}((\pi,\rho),0,a)

\displaystyle:=(\pi,\rho^{\prime})

\displaystyle\textsf{insert}(((m,\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}),\rho),1+n^{\prime},a)

\displaystyle:=((m,\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime},n^{\prime},a)),\rho)

Lemma 5.24.

Given
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime});\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
where

|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}|=n
, and
a\in\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}
, then

\mathcal{O}({\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho},n,a)},{m})=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{m})
.

Proof.

This is immediate because
insert
does not touch the numbers inside.
∎

Lemma 5.25.

Given
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime});\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
where

|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}|=n
,
a\in\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}
and
m\leq n
, then

\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho},n,a)\mid{m}=\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{m},n-m,a)
.

Lemma 5.26.

Given
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime});\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
where

|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}|=n
,
a\in\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}
and
m>n
, then

\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho},n,a)\mid{m}=\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{m}
.

Proof.

The previous two lemmas should be immediate considering the behavior of
insert
.
∎

Lemma 5.27.

Given
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime});\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
where

|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}|=n
,
a\in\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}
and

\overrightarrow{\gamma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Delta}
, then

\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho},n,a)[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]=\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}],n,a[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}])
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
n
.

Case

n=0
, immediate.

Case

n=1+n^{\prime}
, let
((m,\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}),\rho):=\overrightarrow{\rho}
:

\displaystyle\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho},n,a)[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]

\displaystyle=((m,\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime},n^{\prime},a)),\rho)[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]

\displaystyle=((\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}},{m}),\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime},n^{\prime},a)[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{m}]),\rho[\overrightarrow{\gamma}])

(by IH)

\displaystyle=((\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}},{m}),\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{m}],n^{\prime},a[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{m}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}},{n^{\prime}})}])),\rho[\overrightarrow{\gamma}])

\displaystyle=\textsf{insert}(((\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}},{m}),\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{m}]),\rho[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]),1+n^{\prime},a[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{(m+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}},{n^{\prime}}))}])

\displaystyle=\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}],1+n^{\prime},a[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{(1+n^{\prime})})}])

∎

Lemma 5.28.

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime});\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vdash t:T
, given
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime});\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
,
|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}|=n
and
a\in\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}
, then
\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho},n,a))
.

Proof.

This is immediate because
t
does not bind
x
. This can be seen by a
straightforward induction.
∎

Theorem 5.29.

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:S,\Gamma^{\prime});\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vdash t:T
and

\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash s:S
,
Given
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime});\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
where

|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}|=n
,
then

\llbracket t[s/x]\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho},n,\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n})))
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
t
and analyze
n
. Let

\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}:=\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho},n,\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n}))
.

Case

t=y
,

Subcase

n=0
and
y=x
, immediate. Both sides equal

\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})
.

Subcase

n=0
and
y\neq x
, immediate. Both sides look up
\overrightarrow{\rho}
.

Subcase

n>0
, immediate.

Case

t=\texttt{box}\ t^{\prime}
, then

\displaystyle\llbracket t[s/x]\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}[s/x]\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}((1,\overrightarrow{\rho});*)

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}(\textsf{insert}(((1,\overrightarrow{\rho});*),1+n,\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({((1,\overrightarrow{\rho});*)},{1+n})}}(((1,\overrightarrow{\rho});*)\mid{(1+n)})))

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}((1,\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho},n,\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n}))),*)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\texttt{box}\ t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime})

Case

t=\texttt{unbox}_{m}\ t^{\prime}
, then

\displaystyle\llbracket t[s/x]\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}[s/x]\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{m})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{m})[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{m})}\!\!{}]

Now we need to compare
m
and
n
to see if the substitution stays.

Subcase

m\leq n
, then the substitution is still effective,

(by IH)

\displaystyle\llbracket t[s/x]\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{m})}}(\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{m},n-m,\llbracket s\rrbracket_{(\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{m})})\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{m}},{n-m})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{m}\mid{(n-m)})))[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{m})}\!\!{}]

(by
Lemma

5.10
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{m})}}(\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{m},n-m,\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n})))[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{m})}\!\!{}]

(by
Lemma

5.25
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{m})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}\mid{m})[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{m})}\!\!{}]

(notice
\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{m})=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}},{m})
per
Lemma

5.24
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\texttt{unbox}_{m}\ t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime})

Subcase

m>n
, then the substitution is truncated.

\displaystyle\llbracket t[s/x]\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{m})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{m})[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{m})}\!\!{}]

(by
Lemmas

5.24
and
5.26
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}},{m})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}\mid{m})[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}},{m})}\!\!{}]

\displaystyle=\llbracket\texttt{unbox}_{m}\ t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime})

Case

t=\lambda y:S^{\prime}.t^{\prime}
, then

(where
(\pi,\rho):=\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]
)

\displaystyle\llbracket t[s/x]\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle=(\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime},\overrightarrow{\gamma},a:\llbracket S^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}})\mapsto\llbracket t^{\prime}[s/x]\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}((\pi,(\rho,a)))

(by IH)

\displaystyle=(\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime},\overrightarrow{\gamma},a:\llbracket S^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}})\mapsto\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}(\textsf{insert}((\pi,(\rho,a)),n,\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({(\pi,(\rho,a))},{n})}}((\pi,(\rho,a))\mid{n})))

We will clear up this equation a little. First,

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}({(\pi,(\rho,a))},{n})=\mathcal{O}({(\pi,\rho)},{n})=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]},{n})

Next,

\displaystyle\ \llbracket s\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]},{n})}}((\pi,(\rho,a))\mid{n})

(by
Lemma

5.28
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \llbracket s\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]},{n})}}((\pi,\rho)\mid{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \llbracket s\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\mid{n})

(by
Lemmas

5.11
and
5.12
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \llbracket s\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\gamma}},{(\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n}))})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}])

(by naturality)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \llbracket s\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n})[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}]

Last, we can pop the
a
to the top of the environment because
x
must occur
before
y
. This concludes the following equation:

(where
(\pi^{\prime},\rho^{\prime}):=\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}],n,\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n})[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}])
)

\displaystyle\llbracket t[s/x]\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle=(\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime},\overrightarrow{\gamma},a)\mapsto\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}((\pi^{\prime},(\rho^{\prime},a)))

However,

\displaystyle(\pi^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}],n,\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n})[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}])

(by
Lemma

5.27
)

\displaystyle=\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho},n,\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n}))[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]

Therefore

\displaystyle\llbracket t[s/x]\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle=(\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime},\overrightarrow{\gamma},a)\mapsto\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}((\pi^{\prime},(\rho^{\prime},a)))

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\textsf{insert}(\overrightarrow{\rho},n,\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n})))

Case

t=t^{\prime}\ s
, immediate by IH.

∎

Corollary 5.30.

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:S)\vdash t:T
and

\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash s:S
,
Given
(\pi,\rho)\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
,
then

\llbracket t[s/x]\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}((\pi,\rho))=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}((\pi,(\rho,\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}((\pi,\overrightarrow{\rho})))))
.

5.6.
Completeness

We shall move on to prove the completeness of the NbE algorithm given above. The
theorem states

Theorem 5.31.

(completeness) If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx t^{\prime}:T
, then
\textsf{nbe}^{T}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(t)=\textsf{nbe}^{T}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(t^{\prime})
.

5.6.1.
What We Should Do

Ideally, we should prove the completeness theorem through an partial equivalence relation(PER)
between values in the semantics as defined below. However, we take a shortcut here
because we can see that in fact the PER we are about to define induces
equality. Thus, we can just directly go for semantic equality and therefore the
completeness theorem automatically falls into one piece. For the sake of documentary completeness,
we sketch the PER below:

Definition 5.32.

\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(T)
is a relation between
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
:

\displaystyle\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(T)

\displaystyle\subseteq\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}\times\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(B)

\displaystyle:=\{(a,b)\;|\;a=b\}

\displaystyle\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(\square T)

\displaystyle:=\{(a,b)\;|\;\forall\overrightarrow{\gamma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}.(a[\overrightarrow{\gamma}],b[\overrightarrow{\gamma}])\in\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(T)\}

\displaystyle\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(S\longrightarrow T)

\displaystyle:=\{(a,b)\;|\;\forall\overrightarrow{\gamma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Gamma},(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(S).(a(\overrightarrow{\gamma},a^{\prime}),b(\overrightarrow{\gamma},b^{\prime}))\in\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(T)\}

We can verify that this relation is indeed a PER. We generalize
Eq
to both
contexts and context stacks.

Definition 5.33.

\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(\Gamma)
is a relation between
\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
:

\displaystyle\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(\Gamma)

\displaystyle\subseteq\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}\times\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(\cdot)

\displaystyle:=\{(*,*)\}

\displaystyle\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(\Gamma,x:T)

\displaystyle:=\{((\rho,a),(\rho^{\prime},b))\;|\;(\rho,\rho^{\prime})\in\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(\Gamma)\text{ and }(a,b)\in\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(T)\}

Definition 5.34.

\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\Gamma})
is a relation between
\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
:

\displaystyle\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\Gamma})

\displaystyle\subseteq\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}\times\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

\displaystyle\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\epsilon;\Gamma)

\displaystyle:=\{((*,\rho),(*,\rho^{\prime}))\;|\;(\rho,\rho^{\prime})\in\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\Gamma)\}

\displaystyle\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma)

\displaystyle:=\{(((n,\overrightarrow{\rho}),\rho),((n,\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}),\rho^{\prime}))\;|\;(\overrightarrow{\rho},\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime})\in\textsf{Eq}_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{n}}(\overrightarrow{\Gamma})\text{ and }(\rho,\rho^{\prime})\in\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\Gamma)\}

At last, we would work with the following notion of semantic typing:

Definition 5.35.

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t\approx t^{\prime}:T:=\forall\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime},(\overrightarrow{\rho},\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime})\in\textsf{Eq}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\Gamma}).(\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}),\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}))\in Eq_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(T)

5.6.2.
PER Rules

Instead, we simply work with equality in the semantics, so we actually will work with
the following notion:

Definition 5.36.

We define semantic typing and semantic typing equivalence as follows:

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t\approx t^{\prime}:T:=\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T\wedge\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t^{\prime}:T\wedge\forall\overrightarrow{\Delta},\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}.\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t:T:=\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t\approx t:T

We proceed to prove the semantic counterpart for the syntactic rules in

Section

2.4
.

Lemma 5.37.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash s\approx t:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t\approx s:T}

Lemma 5.38.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash s\approx t:T\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t\approx u:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash s\approx u:T}

Proof.

We use the fact that
\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
and
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
are PER.
∎

5.6.3.
Congruence Rules

Lemma 5.39.

\frac{x:T\in\Gamma}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vDash x\approx x:T}

Proof.

Immediate because we are doing the same lookup.
∎

Lemma 5.40.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\vDash t\approx t^{\prime}:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash\texttt{box}\ t\approx\texttt{box}\ t^{\prime}:\square T}

Proof.

In fact, since
\llbracket\square T\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}=\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}
, the premise
immediately induces the conclusion.
∎

Lemma 5.41.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t\approx t^{\prime}:\square T\quad|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|=n}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vDash\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ t\approx\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ t^{\prime}:T^{\prime}}

Proof.

Assume
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}
, then

(assumption)

\displaystyle H_{1}:

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t\approx t^{\prime}:\square T

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}

(by
H_{1}
)

\displaystyle\ \llbracket t\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n})=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n})

Since
\llbracket\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n})[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}\!\!{}]
, we can conclude

\llbracket\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})=\llbracket\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})
.
∎

Lemma 5.42.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:S)\vDash t\approx t^{\prime}:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vDash\lambda x.t\approx\lambda x.t^{\prime}:S\longrightarrow T}

Lemma 5.43.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t\approx t^{\prime}:S\longrightarrow T\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash s\approx s^{\prime}:S}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t\ s\approx t^{\prime}\ s^{\prime}:T}

Proof.

Immediate by premises.
∎

5.6.4.
\beta
Rules

Lemma 5.44.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\vDash t:T\quad|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|=n}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vDash\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ (\texttt{box}\ t)\approx t\{n/0\}:T}

Proof.

Assume
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}
, then

\displaystyle\llbracket\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ (\texttt{box}\ t)\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle=\llbracket\texttt{box}\ t\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n})[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}\!\!{}]

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})};\cdot}((1,\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n}),*)[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}\!\!{}]

(by naturality)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}(((1,\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n}),*)[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})}\!\!{}])

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}(((\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n}),\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n}),*))

(by
Corollary

5.22
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\{n/0\}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

∎

Lemma 5.45.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:S)\vDash t:T\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vDash s:S}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vDash(\lambda x.t)s\approx t[s/x]:T}

Proof.

Assume
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
, let
(\pi,\rho):=\overrightarrow{\rho}
, then

\displaystyle\llbracket(\lambda x.t)s\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}((\pi,(\rho,\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}))))

(by
Corollary

5.30
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t[s/x]\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

∎

5.6.5.
\eta
Rules

Lemma 5.46.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t:\square T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t\approx\texttt{box}\ (\texttt{unbox}_{1}\ t):\square T}

Proof.

Assume
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
, then

\displaystyle\llbracket\texttt{box}\ (\texttt{unbox}_{1}\ t)\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot\mid{1}}(((1,\overrightarrow{\rho}),*)\mid{1})[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({((1,\overrightarrow{\rho}),*)},{1})}\!\!{}]

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{1}\!\!{}]

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

In the last equation, since
\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}):\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}
,

\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{1}\!\!{}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot
is just identity.
∎

Lemma 5.47.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t:S\longrightarrow T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t\approx\lambda x.(t\ x):S\longrightarrow T}

Proof.

Assume
\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
, then

(where
(\pi,\rho):=\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]=\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\circ\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}}]
)

\displaystyle\llbracket\lambda x.(t\ x)\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle=(\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime},\overrightarrow{\gamma},a\in\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}})\mapsto\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}((\pi,(\rho,a)),\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}},a)

(by
Lemma

5.28
)

\displaystyle=(\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime},\overrightarrow{\gamma},a\in\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}})\mapsto\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}(\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}],\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}},a)

(by naturality)

\displaystyle=(\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime},\overrightarrow{\gamma},a\in\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}})\mapsto\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(\overrightarrow{\rho},\overrightarrow{\gamma}\circ\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}},a)

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

∎

5.6.6.
Fundamental Theorem

We have finished all the semantic typing rules. Thus we can establish semantic typing
and semantic typing equivalence.

Theorem 5.48.

(fundamental)

•

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T
, then
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t:T
.

•

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx t^{\prime}:T
, then
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t\approx t^{\prime}:T
.

We next finish the completeness proof.

Proof of
Theorem

5.31
.

Given
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx t^{\prime}:T
, we know
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t\approx t^{\prime}:T
. We plug in

\uparrow^{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
, so we obtain

\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\Gamma}(\uparrow^{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}})=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}(\uparrow^{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}})
, as
well as

\downarrow^{T}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(\uparrow^{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}))=\downarrow^{T}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}(\uparrow^{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}))
.

∎

5.7.
Soundness and Gluing Model

5.7.1.
Gluing Model

A gluing model glues the syntax and the semantics of terms, from which we can extract
the soundness theorem. We write
t\sim a\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
in place of
(t,a)\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
. The model is parameterized by a context stack

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
, so the model moves along unified weakenings, hence forming a Kripke model.

\displaystyle\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle\subseteq\textsf{Exp}\times\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle\llparenthesis B\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle:=\{(t,a)\;|\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx a:B\}

\displaystyle\llparenthesis\square T\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle:=\{(t,a)\;|\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:\square T\text{ and }\forall\overrightarrow{\Delta}.\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\ t\sim a[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\!\!{}]\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}}\}

\displaystyle\llparenthesis S\longrightarrow T\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle:=\{(t,a)\;|\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:S\longrightarrow T\text{ and }\forall\overrightarrow{\gamma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Gamma},s\sim b\in\llparenthesis S\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}.t[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\ s\sim a(\overrightarrow{\gamma},b)\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}\}

Lemma 5.49.

If
t\sim a\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
, then
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T
.

Lemma 5.50.

If
t\sim a\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
, given
\overrightarrow{\gamma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
, then

t[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\sim a[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
T
.

Case

T=B
, immediate because
a\in\textsf{Ne}\ T\ \overrightarrow{\Gamma}
.

Case

T=\square T^{\prime}
, then

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{1}:

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:\square T^{\prime}

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{2}:

\displaystyle\ \forall\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}.\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\ t\sim a[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\!\!{}]\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

(by application of unified weakening)

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash t[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]:\square T^{\prime}

\displaystyle\ \text{assume }\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}

(by
H_{2}
)

\displaystyle H_{3}:

\displaystyle\ \texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\ t\sim a[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\!\!{}]\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\ \text{let }\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}:=\overrightarrow{\gamma};\underbrace{\textsf{id};\cdots;\textsf{id}}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}

(by IH on
H_{3}
with
\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle\ (\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\ t)[\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]\sim a[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\!\!{}][\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

We consider the term and the value:

\displaystyle(\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\ t)[\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]

\displaystyle=\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}\mid{\;|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}])

\displaystyle=\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\gamma}])

\displaystyle a[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\!\!{}][\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]

\displaystyle=a[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\!\!{}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]

\displaystyle=a[(\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}\mid{\;|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|});\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\!\!{}]

\displaystyle=a[\overrightarrow{\gamma};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\!\!{}]

\displaystyle=a[(\overrightarrow{\gamma};\textsf{id})\circ(\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\!\!{})]

\displaystyle=a[\overrightarrow{\gamma}][\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\!\!{}]

Then we can conclude

\displaystyle\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\gamma}])\sim a[\overrightarrow{\gamma}][\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\!\!{}])\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

(by abstraction)

\displaystyle t[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\sim a[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\in\llparenthesis\square T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

Case

T=S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}
, then

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{1}:

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{2}:

\displaystyle\ \forall\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Gamma},s\sim b\in\llparenthesis S\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}.t[\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]\ s\sim a(\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime},b)\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\ \text{assume }\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Delta},s\sim b\in\llparenthesis S\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

(by
H_{2}
)

\displaystyle H_{3}:

\displaystyle\ t[\overrightarrow{\gamma}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]\ s\sim a(\overrightarrow{\gamma}\circ\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime},b)\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\ t[\overrightarrow{\gamma}][\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime}]\ s\sim a[\overrightarrow{\gamma}](\overrightarrow{\gamma}^{\prime},b)\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

(by abstraction)

\displaystyle\ t[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\sim a[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\in\llparenthesis S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

∎

Lemma 5.51.

If
t\sim a\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
and
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx t^{\prime}:T
, then
t^{\prime}~a\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
T
.

Case

T=B
, immediate by transitivity.

Case

T=\square T^{\prime}
, then

(by assumption)

\displaystyle\ \forall\overrightarrow{\Delta}.\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\ t\sim a[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\!\!{}]\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

\displaystyle\ \text{assume }\overrightarrow{\Delta}

\displaystyle\ \texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\ t\sim a[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\!\!{}]\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

(by congruence)

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\ t\approx\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\ t^{\prime}:T^{\prime}

(by IH)

\displaystyle\ \texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\ t^{\prime}\sim a[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\!\!{}]\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

\displaystyle\ t^{\prime}\sim a\in\llparenthesis\square T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

Case

T=S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}
, then

(by assumption)

\displaystyle\ \forall\overrightarrow{\gamma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Gamma},s\sim b\in\llparenthesis S\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}.t[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\ s\sim a(\overrightarrow{\gamma},b)\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

\displaystyle\ \text{assume }\overrightarrow{\gamma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Gamma},s\sim b\in\llparenthesis S\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

(by congruence)

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash t[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\ s\approx t^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\ s:T^{\prime}

(by IH)

\displaystyle\ t^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\ s\sim a(\overrightarrow{\gamma},b)\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

\displaystyle\ t^{\prime}\sim a\in\llparenthesis S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

∎

One last lemma we should prove is that the gluing model implies equivalence after
reification. We need to prove two lemmas mutually:

Lemma 5.52.

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T
and
t
is neutral, then
t\sim\uparrow^{T}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(t)\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
T
.

Case

T=B
, immediate.

Case

T=\square T^{\prime}
, then assuming
\overrightarrow{\Delta}
, we shall prove

\displaystyle\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\ t\sim\uparrow^{T^{\prime}}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot}(\texttt{unbox}_{1}\ t)[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\!\!{}]\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

We perform the following reason on the syntactic term:

(
\eta
rule)

\displaystyle\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\ t

\displaystyle\approx\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\ (\texttt{box}\ (\texttt{unbox}_{1}\ t))

(
\beta
rule)

\displaystyle\approx(\texttt{unbox}_{1}\ t)\{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|/0\}

(as unified weakening)

\displaystyle\approx\texttt{unbox}_{1}\ t[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\!\!{}]

Thus the goal becomes

\displaystyle\texttt{unbox}_{1}\ t[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\!\!{}]\sim\uparrow^{T^{\prime}}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot}(\texttt{unbox}_{1}\ t)[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\!\!{}])\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

by
Lemma

5.51
.

Now we reason forwardly:

(by IH)

\displaystyle\texttt{unbox}_{1}\ t\sim\uparrow^{T^{\prime}}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot}(\texttt{unbox}_{1}\ t)\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

(by
Lemma

5.50
)

\displaystyle\texttt{unbox}_{1}\ t[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\!\!{}]\sim\uparrow^{T^{\prime}}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot}(\texttt{unbox}_{1}\ t)[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\!\!{}]\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

Case

T=S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}
, then assume
\overrightarrow{\gamma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{w}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
and
s\sim b:\llparenthesis S\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
,

\displaystyle\uparrow^{S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(t)(\overrightarrow{\gamma},b)

\displaystyle=\uparrow^{T^{\prime}}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(t[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\ \downarrow^{S}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(b))

By IH, we have

\displaystyle t[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\ \downarrow^{S}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(b)\sim\uparrow^{T^{\prime}}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(t[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\ \downarrow^{S}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}(b))\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

We further obtain from
Lemmas

5.51
and
5.53

\displaystyle t[\overrightarrow{\gamma}]\ s\sim\uparrow^{S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(t)(\overrightarrow{\gamma},b)\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

Therefore
(t,\uparrow^{S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}t)\in\llparenthesis S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
.

∎

Lemma 5.53.

If
t\sim a\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
, then

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx\downarrow^{T}_{\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(a):T
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
T
.

Case

T=B
, immediate.

Case

T=\square T^{\prime}
, then

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{1}:

\displaystyle\ \forall\overrightarrow{\Delta}.\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\ t\sim a[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\!\!{}]\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

(by
H_{1}
with
\overrightarrow{\Delta}
a singleton containing an empty context)

\displaystyle\ \texttt{unbox}_{1}\ t\sim a[\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}};\Uparrow^{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|}\!\!{}]\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot}

(
