\displaystyle\llbracket\texttt{box}\ t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle=\texttt{box}(\llbracket t\rrbracket(\texttt{ext}(\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}))))

\displaystyle\llbracket\texttt{box}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{1}\!\!{}])\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\texttt{box}(\llbracket t\rrbracket(\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{1}\!\!{}\rrbracket(\texttt{ext}(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}))))

(by definition)

\displaystyle=\texttt{box}(\llbracket t\rrbracket(\texttt{ext}(\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}))))

The goal follows immediately by the premises and
Lemma

6.26
.
∎

To prove the congruence for
unbox
, we need two helper lemmas:

Lemma 6.41.

\mathcal{O}({\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})},{n})=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})})

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
n
and
\overrightarrow{\sigma}
. We analyze the valid cases by
inverting
\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})
and
\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})
.

Case

n=0
, immediate.

Case

n=1+n^{\prime}
, then

Subcase

\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\overrightarrow{I}
or
\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\textsf{wk}_{x}
, immediate.

Subcase

\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime},t/x
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}({\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})},{n})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})},{n})

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}},{n})})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})})

Subcase

\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime};\Uparrow^{m}\!\!{}
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}({\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})},{n})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{m})+\mathcal{O}({\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{m})},{n^{\prime}})

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{m})+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{m}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}},{n^{\prime}})})

(by
Lemma

6.5
)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{m+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}},{n^{\prime}})})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})})

Subcase

\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}({\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})},{n})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket(\llbracket\overrightarrow{\delta}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}))},{n})

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\llbracket\overrightarrow{\delta}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}},{n})})

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}},{n})})})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}},{n})})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})})

∎

Lemma 6.42.

\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})\mid{n}=\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})})

Proof.

The same as above, we proceed by induction on
n
and
\overrightarrow{\sigma}
. We analyze the
valid cases by inverting
\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})
and
\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n}
.

Case

n=0
, immediate.

Case

n=1+n^{\prime}
, then

Subcase

\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\overrightarrow{I}
or
\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\textsf{wk}_{x}
, immediate.

Subcase

\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime},t/x
, then

\displaystyle\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})\mid{n}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})\mid{n}

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\mid{n}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}},{n})})

\displaystyle=\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})})

Subcase

\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime};\Uparrow^{m}\!\!{}
, then

\displaystyle\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})\mid{n}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{m})\mid{n}

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\mid{n}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{m}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}},{n})})

\displaystyle=\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\mid{n}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{(m+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}},{n}))})

\displaystyle=\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})})

Subcase

\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}
, then

\displaystyle\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})\mid{n}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket(\llbracket\overrightarrow{\delta}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}))\mid{n}

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\mid{n}\rrbracket(\llbracket\overrightarrow{\delta}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}},{n})})

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\mid{n}\rrbracket(\llbracket\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}},{n})}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}},{n})})}))

\displaystyle=\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\mid{n}\rrbracket(\llbracket\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}},{n})}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}},{n})}))

\displaystyle=\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})})

∎

Lemma 6.43.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vDash t:\square T\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\quad|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|=n\quad|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}|=(\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n}))}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vDash\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx\texttt{unbox}_{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n}]):T}

Proof.

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{1}:

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta}\vDash t:\square T

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{2}:

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{3}:

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\rho}\approx\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrbracket

We compute both sides:

\displaystyle\llbracket\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle=\texttt{unbox}\cdot(\mathcal{O}({\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})},{n}),\llbracket t\rrbracket(\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})\mid{n}))

(by
Lemmas

6.41
and
6.42
)

\displaystyle=\texttt{unbox}\cdot(\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})}),\llbracket t\rrbracket(\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})})))

\displaystyle\llbracket\texttt{unbox}_{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n}])\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\texttt{unbox}\cdot(\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})}),\llbracket t\rrbracket(\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})})))

They agree up to
H_{1}
.
∎

6.7.6.
Substitution Equivalence Rules

The following rules are immediate following the definitions:

Lemma 6.44.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{I}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash\overrightarrow{I}\circ\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime\prime}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash(\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}\circ\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime})\circ\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}\circ(\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\circ\overrightarrow{\sigma}):\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime\prime}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime};\Gamma\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vDash t:T\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma},t/x\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\approx(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}),t[\overrightarrow{\delta}]/x:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime};(\Gamma,x:T)}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vDash t:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vDash\textsf{wk}_{x}\circ(\overrightarrow{\sigma},t/x)\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:T)}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx(\textsf{wk}_{x}\circ\overrightarrow{\sigma}),x[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]/x:\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:T)}

We outline the proof for the following rules:

Lemma 6.45.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\vDash\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}\quad|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|=n}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\vDash(\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{})\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\approx(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ(\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{n}));\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{n})}\!\!{}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\cdot}

Proof.

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{1}:

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{2}:

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\vDash\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{3}:

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\rho}\approx\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket

We compute both sides:

\displaystyle\llbracket(\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{})\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})

\displaystyle=\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\texttt{ext}(\llbracket\overrightarrow{\delta}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})\mid{n}),\mathcal{O}({\llbracket\overrightarrow{\delta}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})},{n}))

(by
Lemmas

6.41
and
6.42
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\texttt{ext}(\llbracket\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{n}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{n})})),\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{n})}))

\displaystyle\llbracket(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ(\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{n}));\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{n})}\!\!{}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\texttt{ext}(\llbracket\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{n}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{n})})),\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{n})}))

The goal follows by the premises.
∎

Lemma 6.46.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot\quad|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|>0}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{1};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{1})}\!\!{}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}

Proof.

(assumption)

\displaystyle H_{1}:

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{2}:

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\rho}\approx\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket

We evaluate the right hand side:

\displaystyle\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{1};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{1})}\!\!{}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\texttt{ext}(\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{1}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{1})}),\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{1})}))

(by
Lemmas

6.41
and
6.42
)

\displaystyle=\texttt{ext}(\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime})\mid{1},\mathcal{O}({\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime})},{1}))

We know

\displaystyle\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})\mid{1}\approx\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime})\mid{1}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Delta}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}({\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})},{1})=\mathcal{O}({\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}^{\prime})},{1})

Since the topmost context of
\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot
is
\cdot
, we have finished the check
and the goal follows immediately.
∎

6.7.7.
Fundamental Theorem

We have proved all semantic rules. We can thus conclude the fundamental theorem.

Theorem 6.47.

(fundamental)

•

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T
, then
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t:T
.

•

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
, then
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
.

•

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx t^{\prime}:T
, then
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t\approx t^{\prime}:T
.

•

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
, then
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
.

We also can show the initial environment is reflexive in
\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket
.

Lemma 6.48.

\uparrow^{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}\approx\uparrow^{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}\in\llbracket\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrbracket

Proof.

Immediate by
Lemma

6.19
and realizability.
∎

Theorem 6.49.

(completeness)
If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx t^{\prime}:T
, then
\textsf{nbe}^{T}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(t)=\textsf{nbe}^{T}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(t^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Given
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx t^{\prime}:T
, we know
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vDash t\approx t^{\prime}:T
. Combining realizability, we
can conclude the goal.
∎

6.8.
Restricted Weakening

In
Section

3.3
, we motivated unified substitutions by first considering the
compositional closure of modal transformations, which turns out has a critical position
in the soundness proof. Luckily, To establish the soundness proof,
we only need to capture the compositional closure of a
special class of modal transformation and ensure that they form a category. The
morphisms in the resulting category are called
restricted weakening
, as they
are semantically a subcategory of unified weakening defined in
Section

5.1
.

Definition 6.50.

A substitution
\overrightarrow{\sigma}
is a restricted weakening, if it satisfies the following
inductively generated predicates:

(1)

if
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{I}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
;

(2)

if
\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:T)
is restricted and

\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\textsf{wk}_{x}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma

for some
m=|\Delta|
;

(3)

if
\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
is restricted and

\overrightarrow{\Delta};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\delta};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot
where
n=|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|
.

This definition essentially characterizes a restricted
\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\textsf{wk}_{x_{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\textsf{wk}_{x_{m}}\circ\overrightarrow{I};\Uparrow^{k_{1}}\!\!{};\cdots;\Uparrow^{k_{n}}\!\!{}
for some
m
,
n
and
k_{i}
. We write
\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Delta}
to denote
\overrightarrow{\sigma}
being restricted.

We consider some basic properties of restricted weakenings It is easy to see that

\overrightarrow{I}
is restricted, as well as
\overrightarrow{I};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}
, the modal transformation in the
\beta

rule for
\square
. If
\overrightarrow{\sigma}
is restricted, then
\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n}
is
also restricted. Moreover, this definition is closed under composition:

Lemma 6.51.

If
\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}
and
\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}
,
then

\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\circ\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

Do induction on the restricted predicate on
\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}
and
then apply the appropriate equivalence rules.
∎

This concludes that restricted weakenings do form a (sub)category. The restrictedness
is important in the soundness proof to simplify the proof.

6.9.
Gluing Model

In order to conclude the soundness of the NbE algorithm, we construct a gluing model,
which intuitively relates a syntactic term with its corresponding semantic value. From
this relation, the soundness theorem can be extracted. Moreover, we need the gluing
relation between related syntactic terms and domain values to be invariant under
restricted weakening as in the PER model. We first need to extract an untyped modal
transformation from a unified substitution:

\displaystyle\texttt{mt}(\_)

\displaystyle:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Rightarrow\overrightarrow{\Delta}\to\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{I})

\displaystyle:=\overrightarrow{1}

\displaystyle\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma},t/x)

\displaystyle:=\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma})

\displaystyle\texttt{mt}(\textsf{wk}_{x})

\displaystyle:=\overrightarrow{1}

\displaystyle\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{})

\displaystyle:=\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma});\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}

\displaystyle\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta})

\displaystyle:=\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma})\circ\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\delta})

This operation simply pulls out the offsets from the input
unified substitution.
Moreover, for convenience, instead of writing
a[\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma})]
, we write
a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]
as
we know
\overrightarrow{\sigma}
must be extracted first to be applied to a domain value. The
notation is justified by the following two lemmas.

We prove the following properties:

Lemma 6.52.

\mathcal{O}({\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma})},{n})=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})

Proof.

We do induction on
\overrightarrow{\sigma}
and
n
.

Case

n=0
, immediate.

Case

n=1+n^{\prime}
, then

Subcase

\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\overrightarrow{I}
,
\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime},t/x
or
\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\textsf{wk}_{x}
are immediate.

Subcase

\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime};\Uparrow^{m}\!\!{}
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}({\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma})},{n})

\displaystyle=m+\mathcal{O}({\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime})},{n^{\prime}})

(by IH)

\displaystyle=m+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}},{n^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})

Subcase

\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}({\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma})},{n})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\delta})},{\mathcal{O}({\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime})},{n})})

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\delta})},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}},{n})})

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}},{n})})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})

∎

Lemma 6.53.

\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n})=\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma})\mid{n}

Proof.

We do induction on
\overrightarrow{\sigma}
and
n
.

Case

n=0
, immediate.

Case

n=1+n^{\prime}
, then

Subcase

\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\overrightarrow{I}
,
\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime},t/x
or

\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\textsf{wk}_{x}
are immediate.

Subcase

\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime};\Uparrow^{m}\!\!{}
, then

\displaystyle\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n})

\displaystyle=\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\mid{n^{\prime}})

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime})\mid{n^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime});\Uparrow^{m}\!\!{}\mid{(1+n^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma})\mid{n}

Subcase

\overrightarrow{\sigma}=\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}
, then

\displaystyle\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n})

\displaystyle=\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\mid{n})\circ\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}},{n})})

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime})\mid{n}\circ\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\delta})\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}},{n})}

(by
Lemma

6.52
)

\displaystyle=\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime})\mid{n}\circ\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\delta})\mid{\mathcal{O}({\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime})},{n})}

\displaystyle=\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\sigma})\mid{n}

∎

The gluing model is constructed to realize a typed candidate space:

\displaystyle\underline{T}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle:=\{(t,\uparrow^{T}(c))\;|\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T\text{ and }\forall\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}.\ \overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Ne}}_{\alpha}(c[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]):T\}

\displaystyle\overline{T}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle:=\{(t,a)\;|\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T\text{ and }\forall\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}.\ \overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Nf}}_{\alpha}(\downarrow^{T}(a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])):T\}

where
\alpha
is uniquely determined by the context stack it is working in. We write

t\sim a\in P
for
(t,a)\in P
when
P
is a gluing relation. We can thus define
the gluing model:

\displaystyle\llparenthesis B\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle:=\underline{B}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle\llparenthesis\square T\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle:=\{(t,a)\;|\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:\square T\text{ and }\forall\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime},\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}.\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\sim\texttt{unbox}\cdot(|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|,a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}\}

\displaystyle\llparenthesis S\longrightarrow T\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle:=\{(t,a)\;|\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:S\longrightarrow T\text{ and }\forall\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma},s\sim b\in\llparenthesis S\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}.t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\ s\sim a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\cdot b\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}\}

Notice that the
S\longrightarrow T
does not need a naturality equality and the
soundness proof does not rely on it.

Lemma 6.54.

If
t\sim a\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
, then
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T
.

Proof.

Immediate by induction on
T
.
∎

Lemma 6.55.

x\sim\uparrow^{T}(\alpha^{-1}(x))\in\underline{T}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma,x:T}

Proof.

Assuming
\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma,x:T
, we do induction on

\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma,x:T
. There can only be two cases:

\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{I}
or
\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\textsf{wk}_{x}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}
for some restricted

\overrightarrow{\delta}
. Thus
\overrightarrow{\sigma}
can only be local weakening. From this observation, we
conclude
\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash x[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx\alpha(\alpha^{-1}(x)):T
holds.
∎

Similar to the PER model, we also need a realizability theorem.

Theorem 6.56 (Realizability).

\underline{T}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}\subseteq\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}\subseteq\overline{T}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
T
.

Case

T=B
,
\underline{B}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}\subseteq\llparenthesis B\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
holds
immediately and
\llparenthesis B\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}\subseteq\overline{B}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
is also easy
because
\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Nf}}
of
B
is just
\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Ne}}
.

Case

T=\square T^{\prime}
, then

Subcase

To show
\underline{\square T^{\prime}}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}\subseteq\llparenthesis\square T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
,

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{1}:

\displaystyle\ \forall\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}.\ \overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Ne}}_{\alpha}(c[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]):\square T^{\prime}

\displaystyle\ \text{assume }\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime},\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma},k:=|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|,\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Delta};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{k}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{k})}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Delta}

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{k}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{k})}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}

(by
H_{1}
and
Lemma

6.53
)

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{k})}\vdash t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ(\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{k})]\approx\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Ne}}_{\alpha}(c[\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ(\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{k})]):\square T^{\prime}

(by congruence)

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\texttt{unbox}_{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{k})}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}][\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{k}])\approx\texttt{unbox}_{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{k})}\ \textsf{R}^{\textsf{Ne}}_{\alpha}(c[\overrightarrow{\sigma}][\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{k}]):T^{\prime}

(use
Lemma

6.52
)

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\texttt{unbox}_{k}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])[\overrightarrow{\delta}]\approx\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Ne}}_{\alpha}(\texttt{unbox}(k,c[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])[\overrightarrow{\delta}]):T^{\prime}

(by abstraction)

\displaystyle\ \texttt{unbox}_{k}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\sim\texttt{unbox}(k,c[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\in\underline{T^{\prime}}_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

(by IH)

\displaystyle\ \texttt{unbox}_{k}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\sim\texttt{unbox}(k,c[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

(by abstraction)

\displaystyle\ t\sim\uparrow^{\square T^{\prime}}(c)\in\llparenthesis\square T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

Subcase

To show
\llparenthesis\square T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}\subseteq\overline{\square T^{\prime}}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
,

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{2}:

\displaystyle\ \forall\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime},\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}.\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\sim\texttt{unbox}\cdot(|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|,a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\ \text{assume }\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}

(by
H_{2}
, letting
\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}=\cdot
)

\displaystyle\ \texttt{unbox}_{1}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\sim\texttt{unbox}\cdot(1,a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}

(by IH)

\displaystyle H_{3}:

\displaystyle\ \texttt{unbox}_{1}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\sim\texttt{unbox}\cdot(1,a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\in\overline{T^{\prime}}_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}

(by
H_{3}
)

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot\vdash\texttt{unbox}_{1}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\approx\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Nf}}_{\alpha}(\downarrow^{T^{\prime}}(\texttt{unbox}\cdot(1,a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]))):T^{\prime}

(congruence)

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash\texttt{box}\ (\texttt{unbox}_{1}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]))\approx\texttt{box}\ (\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Nf}}_{\alpha}(\downarrow^{T^{\prime}}(\texttt{unbox}\cdot(1,a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])))):\square T^{\prime}

(
\eta
expansion)

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Nf}}_{\alpha}(\downarrow^{\square T^{\prime}}(a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])):\square T^{\prime}

(by abstraction)

\displaystyle\ t\sim a\in\overline{\square T^{\prime}}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

Case

, then

Subcase

,

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{4}:

\displaystyle\ \forall\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}.\ \overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Ne}}_{\alpha}(c[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]):S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}

\displaystyle\ \text{assume }\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma},s\sim b\in\llparenthesis S\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}},\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Delta}

)

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\vdash t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}]\approx\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Ne}}_{\alpha}(c[\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}]):S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}

(by IH)

\displaystyle H_{5}:

\displaystyle\ s\sim b\in\overline{S}_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

(by
H_{5}
)

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\vdash s[\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}]\approx\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Nf}}_{\alpha}(\downarrow^{S}(b[\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}])):S

(by congruence)

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\vdash t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}]\ s[\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}]\approx\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Ne}}_{\alpha}(c[\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}])\ \textsf{R}^{\textsf{Nf}}_{\alpha}(\downarrow^{S}(b[\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}])):T^{\prime}

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\vdash(t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\ s)[\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}]\approx\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Ne}}_{\alpha}((c[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\ \downarrow^{S}(b))[\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}]):T^{\prime}

(by abstraction)

\displaystyle\ t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\ s\sim\uparrow^{T^{\prime}}(c[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\ \downarrow^{S}(b))\in\underline{T^{\prime}}_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

(by IH)

\displaystyle\ t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\ s\sim\uparrow^{T^{\prime}}(c[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\ \downarrow^{S}(b))\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

(by abstraction)

\displaystyle\ t\sim\uparrow^{S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}}(c)\in\llparenthesis S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

Subcase

To show
\llparenthesis S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}\subseteq\overline{S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
,

\displaystyle H_{6}:

\displaystyle\ \forall\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma},s\sim b\in\llparenthesis S\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}.t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\ s\sim a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\cdot b\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

\displaystyle\ \text{assume }\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}

(note
\overline{\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}}=\overline{\overrightarrow{\sigma}}
)

\displaystyle\ \text{let }\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:=\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\textsf{wk}_{x}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta,x:S\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}

(for some

, by

6.55
)

\displaystyle\ x\sim z\in\underline{S}_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta,x:S}

(by IH)

\displaystyle\ x\sim z\in\llparenthesis S\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta,x:S}

(by
H_{6}
)

\displaystyle\ t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}]\ x\sim a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\cdot z\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta,x:S}

(by IH)

\displaystyle H_{7}:

\displaystyle\ t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}]\ x\sim a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\cdot z\in\overline{T^{\prime}}_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta,x:S}

(by
H_{7}
)

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta,x:S\vdash t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}]\ x\approx\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Nf}}_{\alpha[z\mapsto x]}(\downarrow^{T^{\prime}}(a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\cdot z)):T^{\prime}

(by congruence)

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta\vdash\lambda x.t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\textsf{wk}_{x}]\ x\approx\lambda x.\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Nf}}_{\alpha[z\mapsto x]}(\downarrow^{T^{\prime}}(a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\cdot z)):S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta\vdash t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Nf}}_{\alpha}(\downarrow^{S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}}(a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])):S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}

(by abstraction)

\displaystyle\ t\sim a\in\overline{S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

∎

We need this lemma stating that the gluing model respects syntactic equivalence:

Lemma 6.57.

If
t\sim a\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
and
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t^{\prime}\approx t:T
, then

t^{\prime}\sim a\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
.

Proof.

We do induction on
T
.

Case

T=B
, immediate by transitivity.

Case

T=\square T^{\prime}
, then

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{1}:

\displaystyle\ \forall\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime},\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}.\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\sim\texttt{unbox}\cdot(|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|,a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\ \text{assume }\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime},\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}

(by congruence)

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\vdash\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\ (t^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\approx\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]):T^{\prime}

(by IH)

\displaystyle\ \texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|}\ (t^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\sim\texttt{unbox}\cdot(|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|,a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

(by abstraction)

\displaystyle\ t^{\prime}\sim a\in\llbracket\square T^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

Case

T=S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}
, then

\displaystyle H_{2}:

\displaystyle\ \forall\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma},s\sim b\in\llparenthesis S\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}.t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\ s\sim a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\cdot b\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

\displaystyle\ \text{assume }\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma},s\sim b\in\llparenthesis S\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash s:S

(by congruence)

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash t^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\ s\approx t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\ s:T^{\prime}

(by IH)

\displaystyle\ t^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\ s\sim a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\cdot b\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

(by abstraction)

\displaystyle\ t^{\prime}\sim a\in\llbracket S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

∎

We would also like to reveal the Kripke structure in the gluing model:

Lemma 6.58.

If
t\sim a\in\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
and
\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Delta}
, then

t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\sim a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
.

Proof.

We analyze
T
. Notice that this proof does not use IH.

Case

T=B
, immediate because
\overrightarrow{\sigma}
is closed under composition.

Case

T=\square T^{\prime}
, then

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{1}:

\displaystyle\ \forall\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime},\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Delta}.\texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}|}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\delta}])\sim\texttt{unbox}\cdot(|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}|,a[\overrightarrow{\delta}])\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\ \text{assume }\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime},\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma}

(by
H_{1}
)

\displaystyle\ \texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}|}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}])\sim\texttt{unbox}\cdot(|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}|,a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}])\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}}

(by
Lemma

6.57
)

\displaystyle\ \texttt{unbox}_{|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}|}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}][\overrightarrow{\delta}])\sim\texttt{unbox}\cdot(|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}|,a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}][\overrightarrow{\delta}])\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}}

(by abstraction)

\displaystyle\ t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\sim a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\in\llparenthesis\square T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

Case

T=S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}
, then

\displaystyle H_{2}:

\displaystyle\ \forall\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Delta},s\sim b\in\llparenthesis S\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}.t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}]\ s\sim a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}]\cdot b\in\llparenthesis T^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\ \text{assume }\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Gamma},s\sim b\in\llparenthesis S\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

(by abstraction)

\displaystyle\ t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\sim a[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\in\llbracket S\longrightarrow T^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
