We generalize gluing to contexts and context stacks:

\displaystyle\llparenthesis\epsilon;\Gamma\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

\displaystyle:=\{(\overrightarrow{\sigma},\overrightarrow{\rho})\;|\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\epsilon;\Gamma\text{ and }\forall x:T\in\Gamma.x[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\sim\rho(x)\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}\text{ where }(k,\rho):=\overrightarrow{\rho}(0)\}

\displaystyle\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

\displaystyle:=\{(\overrightarrow{\sigma},\overrightarrow{\rho})\;|\;\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\text{ and }\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{1})=k

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\text{ and }\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{1}\sim\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{1}\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{k}}\text{ and }\forall x:T\in\Gamma.x[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\sim\rho(x)\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}\text{ where }(k,\rho):=\overrightarrow{\rho}(0)\}

We need the follow lemmas to understand operations on
\overrightarrow{\sigma}\sim\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
.

Lemma 6.59.

If
\overrightarrow{\sigma}\sim\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
, then
\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n})
.

Proof.

Immediate by induction on
n
.
∎

Lemma 6.60.

If
\overrightarrow{\sigma}\sim\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
, then
\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n}\sim\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n}\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\mid{n}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})}}
.

Proof.

Immediate by induction on
n
.
∎

Lemma 6.61.

If
\overrightarrow{\sigma}\sim\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
and
\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
, then

\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\sim\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
.

Proof.

Immediate by induction on
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
and apply
Lemma

6.57
when
appropriate.
∎

Lemma 6.62.

If
\overrightarrow{\sigma}\sim\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
, given
\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Delta}
, then

\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\sim\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\delta}]\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Immediate by induction on
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
and apply
Lemmas

6.57
and
6.58
when
appropriate.
∎

Finally, we can define the semantic judgment:

Definition 6.63.

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash t:T

\displaystyle:=\forall\overrightarrow{\sigma}\sim\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}.t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\sim\llbracket t\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\forall\overrightarrow{\sigma}\sim\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}.\overrightarrow{\delta}\circ\overrightarrow{\sigma}\sim\llbracket\overrightarrow{\delta}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

6.10.
Soundness

6.10.1.
Semantic Typing Rules

Lemma 6.64.

\frac{x:T\in\Gamma}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\Vdash x:T}

Proof.

Immediate.
∎

Lemma 6.65.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\Vdash t:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash\texttt{box}\ t:\square T}

Proof.

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{1}:

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\Vdash t:T

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{2}:

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\sigma}\sim\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

\displaystyle\ \text{assume }\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime},\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Delta},\text{let }k:=|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}|

(by definition)

\displaystyle H_{3}:

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{1}\!\!{}\sim\texttt{ext}(\overrightarrow{\rho})\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}

(by
H_{1}
and
H_{3}
)

\displaystyle H_{4}:

\displaystyle\ t[\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{1}\!\!{}]\sim\llbracket t\rrbracket(\texttt{ext}(\overrightarrow{\rho}))\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}

By computation, we have

\displaystyle\texttt{unbox}_{k}\ (\texttt{box}\ t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}][\overrightarrow{\delta}])

\displaystyle\approx t[\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{1}\!\!{}][\overrightarrow{\delta};\Uparrow^{1}\!\!{}][\overrightarrow{I};\Uparrow^{k}\!\!{}]

\displaystyle\approx t[\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{1}\!\!{}][\overrightarrow{\delta};\Uparrow^{k}\!\!{}]:T

as well as

\displaystyle\texttt{unbox}\cdot(k,\llbracket\texttt{box}\ t\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})[\overrightarrow{\delta}])

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket(\texttt{ext}(\overrightarrow{\rho}))[\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\delta});\Uparrow^{1}\!\!{}][\overrightarrow{1};\Uparrow^{k}\!\!{}]

\displaystyle=\llbracket t\rrbracket(\texttt{ext}(\overrightarrow{\rho}))[\texttt{mt}(\overrightarrow{\delta});\Uparrow^{k}\!\!{}]

Thus
H_{4}
is what we want after applying
Lemmas

6.57
and
6.58
.

(by abstraction)

\displaystyle\texttt{box}\ t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\sim\llbracket\texttt{box}\ t\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})\in\llparenthesis\square T\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}

(by abstraction)

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash\texttt{box}\ t:\square T

∎

Lemma 6.66.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash t:\square T\quad|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|=n}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Vdash\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ t:T}

Proof.

Immediate by definition of
\llparenthesis\square T\rrparenthesis
and
Lemma

6.60
.
∎

Lemma 6.67.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma,x:S\Vdash t:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\Vdash\lambda x.t:S\longrightarrow T}

Proof.

Assume
\overrightarrow{\sigma}\sim\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}}
,
\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}
,

\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{r}\overrightarrow{\Delta}
and
s\sim b\in\llparenthesis S\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}
. Then we should
prove that

\displaystyle(\lambda x.t)[\overrightarrow{\sigma}][\overrightarrow{\delta}]\ s\sim\llbracket t\rrbracket(\texttt{ext}(\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\delta}],x,b))\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

We need to construct
\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma,x:S\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}
from what we have. We
claim

\displaystyle(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}),s/x\sim\texttt{ext}(\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\delta}],x,b)\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma,x:S\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

by
Lemma

6.62
. Therefore

\displaystyle t[(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}),s/x]\sim\llbracket t\rrbracket(\texttt{ext}(\overrightarrow{\rho}[\overrightarrow{\delta}],x,b))\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

Comparing the goal and what we have, we can conlude by
Lemma

6.57

using congruence rules and
\beta
equivalence for
\lambda
.
∎

The following rules are proved just by following the definitions:

Lemma 6.68.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash t:S\longrightarrow T\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash s:S}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash t\ s:T}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\Vdash t:T\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]:T}\par\frac{}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash\overrightarrow{I}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash t:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma},t/x:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};(\Gamma,x:T)}\par\frac{}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:T)\Vdash\textsf{wk}_{x}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\Vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}}

Proof.

Immediate by just applying the premises.
∎

At last, we consider the following rule:

Lemma 6.69.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\quad|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}|=n}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\Vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}

Proof.

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{1}:

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}

(by assumption)

\displaystyle H_{2}:

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\delta}\sim\overrightarrow{\rho}\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

(by
H_{2}
)

\displaystyle H_{3}:

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{n}\sim\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n}\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{n})}}

(by
H_{1}
)

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ(\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{n})\sim\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n})\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Delta}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{n})}}

(by definition and
Lemma

6.59
)

\displaystyle\ (\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ(\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{n}));\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{n})}\!\!{}\sim\texttt{ext}(\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho}\mid{n}),\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\rho}},{n}))\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

Notice that the left hand side has

\displaystyle(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ(\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{n}));\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{n})}\!\!{}\approx(\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{})\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot

Thus we conclude by
Lemma

6.61

\displaystyle(\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{})\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\sim\llbracket\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}\rrbracket(\overrightarrow{\rho})\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

and hence the goal by abstraction.
∎

6.10.2.
Fundamental Theorem

Since we have proven the semantic typing rules, we can conclude the fundamental
theorem:

Theorem 6.70.

(fundamental)

•

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T
, then
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash t:T
.

•

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
, then
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\Vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
.

We also need to relate
\overrightarrow{\textsf{id}}
and
\uparrow^{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
:

Lemma 6.71.

\overrightarrow{I}\sim\uparrow^{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}\in\llparenthesis\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
.

Proof.

Immediate by induction on
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
and apply
Lemma

6.55
.
∎

Theorem 6.72.

(soundness) If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T
, then
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx\textsf{nbe}^{T}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(t):T
.

Proof.

Applying the fundamental theorem, we have
t[\overrightarrow{I}]\sim\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}(\uparrow^{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}})\in\llparenthesis T\rrparenthesis_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}
. We conclude the goal
by applying
Lemma

6.57
and realizability.
∎

6.11.
Details on De Bruijn Indices

In the formulation above,
\alpha
in the readback functions is somewhat mysterious:
indeed, our naming convention is mysterious after all. On the flip side, if we
concretize the naming convention, then we can say more about
\alpha
. In this section,
we consider names as de Bruijn indices.

With de Bruijn indices, given
\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:T,\Delta)
,
x
is
represented by the number
|\Delta|
in the syntax. As discussed in
Section

6.5
,
in the domain,
x
corresponds to
|\Gamma|
in the domain. Therefore, given any
variable
x
in the current world, its de Bruijn index
n
and its domain variable
m
must sum to

|\Gamma,x:T,\Delta|-1
. We can use this relation to keep
track of conversion between two representations. This suggests us to rewrite the
readback functions to

\displaystyle\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Nf}}

\displaystyle:\overrightarrow{\mathbb{N}}\rightharpoonup D^{\textsf{Nf}}\rightharpoonup\textsf{Nf}

\displaystyle\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Nf}}_{\overrightarrow{n}}(\downarrow^{\square T}(a))

\displaystyle:=\texttt{box}\ \textsf{R}^{\textsf{Nf}}_{\overrightarrow{n};0}(\downarrow^{T}(\texttt{unbox}\cdot(1,a)))

\displaystyle\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Nf}}_{\overrightarrow{n};n}(\downarrow^{S\longrightarrow T}(a))

\displaystyle:=\lambda x.\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Nf}}_{\overrightarrow{n};n+1}(\downarrow^{T}(a\cdot\uparrow^{S}(n)))

\displaystyle\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Nf}}_{\overrightarrow{n}}(\downarrow^{B}(\uparrow^{B}(c)))

\displaystyle:=\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Ne}}_{\overrightarrow{n}}(c)

\displaystyle\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Ne}}

\displaystyle:\overrightarrow{\mathbb{N}}\rightharpoonup D^{\textsf{Ne}}\rightharpoonup\textsf{Ne}

(if
n-z-1<0
, we return
0
instead)

\displaystyle\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Ne}}_{\overrightarrow{n};n}(z)

\displaystyle:=n-z-1

\displaystyle\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Ne}}_{\overrightarrow{n}}(c\ d)

\displaystyle:=\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Ne}}_{\overrightarrow{n}}(c)\ \textsf{R}^{\textsf{Nf}}_{\overrightarrow{n}}(d)

\displaystyle\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Ne}}_{\overrightarrow{n}}(\texttt{unbox}(k,c))

\displaystyle:=\texttt{unbox}_{k}\ \textsf{R}^{\textsf{Ne}}_{\overrightarrow{n}\mid{k}}(c)

Instead of some partial function
\mathbb{N}\rightharpoonup\textsf{Var}
, we use more concrete
\overrightarrow{\mathbb{N}}
to keep track of the length of each context in the context stack. Since there must
be at least one context in the stack, we can assume all
\overrightarrow{\mathbb{N}}
involved must be
nonempty. We use this knowledge in the function case, in which we increment the
topmost number in the number stack by one, and the variable case, in which we compute
a de Bruijn index from a domain variable. In the
\square T
case, since we are
adding an empty context to the stack, the number stack also grows by one element which
is
0
, reflecting the newly added context is empty. Symmetrically, in the
unbox

case, we should truncate the number stack in the recursive call to
\overrightarrow{n}|_{k}
to
ensure that variables in the same worlds correspond.

With de Bruijn indices, we can redefine
nbe
as

Definition 6.73.

The NbE algorithm is defined to be

\displaystyle\textsf{nbe}_{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}^{T}(t):=\textsf{R}^{\textsf{Nf}}_{\texttt{map}(\Gamma\mapsto|\Gamma|,\overrightarrow{\Gamma})}(\downarrow^{T}(\llbracket t\rrbracket(\uparrow^{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}})))

In this definition, we can see that indeed every context stack
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
uniquely
determines a number stack as
\texttt{map}(\Gamma\mapsto|\Gamma|,\overrightarrow{\Gamma})
, which indeed
satisfies our earlier claim.

The completeness and soundness proofs stay essentially the same. Minor adjustments are
needed due to change of readback functions, but they should be abstracted away
by realizability theorems. Our formalization is based on de Bruijn indices and fully
checked by Agda. It can be found at

https://gitlab.com/JasonHuZS/practice/-/tree/master/Unbox
.

7.
Mint
: A Dependently Typed Extension

In this section, we extend the type theories discussed in the previous sections with
dependent types. In the next section, we examine its meta-theory. In the sections
after this, we give its NbE algorithm and the completeness and soundness proofs. We
introduce
Mint
, a
M
odal
IN
tuitionistic
T
ype theory.
It is an extension of Martin-Löf’s intuitionistic type theory
with
\square
. It supports large elimination and a full cumulative
universe hierarchy.

(given natural numbers,
\mathbb{N}
)

\displaystyle i,j,k,l,m,n

(variables,
Var
)

\displaystyle x,y

(Terms,
Trm
)

\displaystyle s,t,u,M,S,T

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\ x

(types)

\displaystyle\;|\;\texttt{Nat}\;|\;\square T\;|\;\Pi(x:S).T

(universe)

\displaystyle\;|\;\texttt{Ty}_{i}

(natural numbers
Nat
)

\displaystyle\;|\;\textsf{zero}\;|\;\textsf{succ }t\;|\;\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}\ (x.M)\ s\ (x,y.u)\ t

(box)

\displaystyle\;|\;\texttt{box}\ t\;|\;\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ t

(dependent functions)

\displaystyle\;|\;\lambda x.t\;|\;s\ t

(substitution)

\displaystyle\;|\;t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]

(Unified substitution,
Substs
)

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\sigma},\overrightarrow{\delta}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\ \overrightarrow{I}\;|\;\overrightarrow{\sigma},t/x\;|\;\textsf{wk}_{x}\;|\;\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}\;|\;\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}

(Contexts,
Ctx
)

\displaystyle\Gamma,\Delta,\Phi

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\ \cdot\;|\;\Gamma,x:T

(Context
stacks,
\overrightarrow{\textsf{Ctx}}
)

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma},\overrightarrow{\Delta}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\ \epsilon\;|\;\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma

(Normal form (
Nf
))

\displaystyle w,W

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\ v\;|\;\texttt{Nat}\;|\;\texttt{Ty}_{i}\;|\;\square W\;|\;\Pi(x:W).W^{\prime}

\displaystyle\;|\;\textsf{zero}\;|\;\textsf{succ }w\;|\;\texttt{box}\ w\;|\;\lambda x.w

(Neutral form (
Ne
))

\displaystyle v,V

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\ x\;|\;\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}\ (x.w)\ w^{\prime}\ (x,y.w^{\prime\prime})\ v\;|\;\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ v\;|\;v\ w

Since we are working in a dependently typed system, the distinction between terms and
types are gone. We use
S
and
T
to represent terms that are intended to be
types.
s
,
t
and
u
are terms of some types. We use
M
exclusively for motives,
which are needed for the elimination principle of object-level natural numbers. In
general, we always use upper letters to represent those intended to be types and lower
letters to represent those intended to be terms. Otherwise, one finds many
resemblances from the simply typed case. We next move on to defining the rules.

In the judgments below, we use

\displaystyle\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}

to denote well-formedness of the context stack
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
. We use

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T

to denote a term
t
has type
T
in the context stack
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
. When
T
is
\texttt{Ty}_{n}

for some
n
, it denotes the formation of
t
as a type. Moreover, we assume
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}

to have length at least
1
. When stating the well-formedness of a type, we sometimes
do not care about the universe level it resides in, and thus we use

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash T:=\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}

for some
i
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx t^{\prime}:T

denotes terms
t
and
t^{\prime}
of type
T
are equivalent in the context stack

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
. Similarly, we define

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash T\approx T^{\prime}:=\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash T\approx T^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{i}

Through this judgment, we can judge whether two types are equivalent. This
can be generalized to the whole context stack:

\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\approx\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}

The following judgments have similar meanings:

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\par\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}

except that they are for substitutions.

We sometimes use iterative syntax for
\Pi
types. That is,

\displaystyle\Pi(x_{1}:S_{1},\cdots,x_{n}:S_{n}).T:=\Pi(x_{1}:S_{1}).\cdots\Pi(x_{n}:S_{n}).T

7.1.
Well-formedness of Context Stacks

We use the judgment

\displaystyle\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}

to denote that the context stack
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
is well-formed. It is
given by the following judgments:

\frac{}{\vdash\epsilon;\cdot}\par\frac{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot}\par\frac{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:T)}

Note that the base case means that we must start with a singleton stack containing the
empty context. Then we can choose to move to the next world or add a type to the local
world. These rules capture the assumption we mentioned before that well-formed
context stacks are always non-empty:

Lemma 7.1.

If
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
, then
|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}|>0
.

This means that we can always assume there is a top-level context whenever necessary.

We also need the equivalence between contexts because this system has explicit substitutions:

\frac{}{\vdash\epsilon;\cdot\approx\epsilon;\cdot}\par\frac{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\approx\overrightarrow{\Delta}}{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\approx\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}\par\frac{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\approx\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash T\approx T^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\quad\quad\boxed{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta\vdash T\approx T^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\quad\boxed{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\quad\boxed{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta\vdash T^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}}{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:T)\approx\overrightarrow{\Delta};(\Delta,x:T^{\prime})}

We surround some premises by boxes because they are not technically necessary. We add
them here in order to prove some structural properties and after that we will be able
to remove these boxed premises. Some boxed premises are also needed much later when we
establish the soundness proof of the NbE algorithm. We will use them until the end of discussion about
syntactic properties and then they will be removed.

7.2.
Conversion

We admit the conversion rule which effectively take quotient on types over
convertibility:

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash T\approx T^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T^{\prime}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx t^{\prime}:T\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash T\approx T^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx t^{\prime}:T^{\prime}}

7.3.
Substitution

Following the simply typed case, the dependently typed system also uses explicit
substitutions. This requires truncation and truncation offset defined as in

Section

6.1
. Next we define the rules:

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash t:T\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]:T[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash t\approx t^{\prime}:T\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx t^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}]:T[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]}

The following judgments specify well-formed unified substitutions. Adjustments are
made due to the dependently typed nature:

\frac{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{I}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}\par\frac{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma,x:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma,x:T\vdash\textsf{wk}_{x}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma},t/x:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};(\Gamma,x:T)}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\quad\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\quad|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}|=n}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}}

The following are the congruence rules:

\frac{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{I}\approx\overrightarrow{I}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}\par\frac{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma,x:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma,x:T\vdash\textsf{wk}_{x}\approx\textsf{wk}_{x}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx t^{\prime}:T[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma},t/x\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma},t^{\prime}/x:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};(\Gamma,x:T)}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\quad\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\quad|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}|=n}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\delta}\approx\overrightarrow{\delta}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}}

We also need a conversion rule for substitution:

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\quad\vdash\overrightarrow{\Delta}\approx\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\quad\vdash\overrightarrow{\Delta}\approx\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}

Application of substitution satisfies the following equivalence:

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t[\overrightarrow{I}]\approx t:T}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\vdash t:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}]\approx t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}][\overrightarrow{\delta}]:T[\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}]}

Substitution satisfies the PER rules (omitted) and the following algebraic rules:

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{I}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{I}\circ\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime\prime}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash(\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}\circ\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime})\circ\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}\circ(\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\circ\overrightarrow{\sigma}):\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime\prime}}

We next consider composition:

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime};\Gamma\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vdash t:T[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma},t/x\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\approx(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}),t[\overrightarrow{\delta}]/x:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime};(\Gamma,x:T)}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\quad\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\textsf{wk}_{x}\circ(\overrightarrow{\sigma},t/x)\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}\quad|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|=n\quad\boxed{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\vdash(\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{})\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\approx(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{n});\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{n})}\!\!{}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\cdot}

At last, we consider the extensionality principles which stipulates how syntax should
interact:

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:T)}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx(\textsf{wk}_{x}\circ\overrightarrow{\sigma}),x[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]/x:\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:T)}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot\quad|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|>0}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{1};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{1})}\!\!{}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}

7.4.
Variables

Variables are well-typed if they are bound:

\frac{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\quad x:T\in\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash x:T}\par\frac{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\quad x:T\in\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash x\approx x:T}\par\frac{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:T)\quad y:T^{\prime}\in\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash y[\textsf{wk}_{x}]\approx y:T^{\prime}[\textsf{wk}_{x}]}

The binding judgment
x:T\in\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma
must be adjusted due to dependent types:

\frac{}{x:T[\textsf{wk}_{x}]\in\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma,x:T}\par\frac{x:T\in\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma}{x:T[\textsf{wk}_{y}]\in\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma,y:T^{\prime}}

Notice that this judgment never looks up beyond the topmost context, so only variables
in the current world can be directly accessed.

The following specify the application of substitution to variables:

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash x[\overrightarrow{\sigma},t/x]\approx t:T[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash T^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\quad\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T^{\prime}[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\quad y:T\in\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash y[\overrightarrow{\sigma},t/x]\approx y[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]:T[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]}

7.5.
\Pi
Types

\Pi
types generalize function spaces by allowing return types to depend on the input
arguments. For each type to be defined from now on, we will formulate the formation,
construction and elimination rules, as well as the equivalence rules.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash S:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:S)\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash\Pi(x:S).T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\par\frac{\boxed{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash S:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:S)\vdash t:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash\lambda x.t:\Pi(x:S).T}\par\frac{\boxed{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash S:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\quad\boxed{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:S)\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:\Pi(x:S).T\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash s:S}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\ s:T[\overrightarrow{I},s/x]}

Since we will be working on cumulative universes, it is sufficient to stipulate that
S

and
T
have the same universe level in the formation rule.

Now we define the equivalence rules. Among the congruence rules, the following one is
interesting as it requires a redundant premise:

\frac{\boxed{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash S:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash S\approx S^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};(\Gamma,x:S)\vdash T\approx T^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash\Pi(x:S).T\approx\Pi(x:S^{\prime}).T^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}

We omit the rest of congruence rules. Moreover, we
have
\beta
and
\eta
equivalences:

\frac{\boxed{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash S:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\quad\boxed{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:S)\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\quad\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:S)\vdash t:T\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash s:S}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash(\lambda x.t)\ s\approx t[\overrightarrow{I},x:s]:T[\overrightarrow{I},x:s]}\par\frac{\boxed{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash S:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\quad\boxed{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:S)\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\quad\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:\Pi(x:S).T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx\lambda x.t[\textsf{wk}_{x}]\ x:\Pi(x:S).T}

Given
\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta
and
\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}
, we define

\displaystyle q(\overrightarrow{\sigma},x)

\displaystyle:\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:T[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\Rightarrow\overrightarrow{\Delta};(\Delta,x:T)

\displaystyle q(\overrightarrow{\sigma},x)

\displaystyle:=(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\textsf{wk}_{x}),x/x

So we can stipulate that substitutions apply as follows:

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta\quad\quad\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta\vdash S:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\quad\overrightarrow{\Delta};(\Delta,x:S)\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash\Pi(x:S).T[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx\Pi(x:S[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]).(T[q(\overrightarrow{\sigma},x)]):\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta\quad\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta,x:S\vdash t:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash\lambda x.t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx\lambda x.(t[q(\overrightarrow{\sigma},x)]):\Pi(x:S).T[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]}\par\frac{\boxed{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta\vdash S:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\quad\boxed{\overrightarrow{\Delta};(\Delta,x:S)\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta\quad\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta\vdash s:\Pi(x:S).T\quad\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta\vdash t:S}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash s\ t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx(s[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]):T[\overrightarrow{\sigma},x:t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]]}

7.6.
Natural Numbers

We use natural numbers as a representative for algebraic data types. Generalization to
actual inductive data types should follow very closely to MLTT.

\frac{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\texttt{Nat}:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\par\frac{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\textsf{zero}:\texttt{Nat}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:\texttt{Nat}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\textsf{succ }t:\texttt{Nat}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat})\vdash M:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash s:M[\overrightarrow{I},\textsf{zero}/x]\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat},y:M)\vdash u:M[(\textsf{wk}_{x}\circ\textsf{wk}_{y}),\textsf{succ }x/x]\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash t:\texttt{Nat}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}\ (x.M)\ s\ (x,y.u)\ t:M[\overrightarrow{I},t/x]}

One might have detected from the formation rule that we are forming a cumulative
universe. This allows us to reason about a simpler type theory. We will expand the
discussion when talking about the universes. Again, we omit the
congruence rules as they are immediate. The
\beta
equivalence rules are

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat})\vdash M:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash s:M[\overrightarrow{I},\textsf{zero}/x]\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat},y:M)\vdash u:M[(\textsf{wk}_{x}\circ\textsf{wk}_{y}),\textsf{succ }x/x]}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}\ (x.M)\ s\ (x,y.u)\ \textsf{zero}\approx s:M[\overrightarrow{I},\textsf{zero}/x]}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat})\vdash M:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash s:M[\overrightarrow{I},\textsf{zero}/x]\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat},y:M)\vdash u:M[(\textsf{wk}_{x}\circ\textsf{wk}_{y}),\textsf{succ }x/x]\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash t:\texttt{Nat}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}\ (x.M)\ s\ (x,y.u)\ (\textsf{succ }t)\approx u[\overrightarrow{I},t/x,\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}\ (x.M)\ s\ (x,y.u)\ t/y]:M[\overrightarrow{I},\textsf{succ }t/x]}

Next we define applications of substitutions:

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\texttt{Nat}[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx\texttt{Nat}:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\textsf{zero}[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx\textsf{zero}:\texttt{Nat}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\quad\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash t:\texttt{Nat}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\textsf{succ }t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx\textsf{succ }(t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]):\texttt{Nat}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta\quad\overrightarrow{\Delta};(\Delta,x:\texttt{Nat})\vdash M:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\quad\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta\vdash s:M[\overrightarrow{I},\textsf{zero}/x]\quad\overrightarrow{\Delta};(\Delta,x:\texttt{Nat},y:M)\vdash u:M[(\textsf{wk}_{x}\circ\textsf{wk}_{y}),\textsf{succ }x/x]\quad\overrightarrow{\Delta};\Delta\vdash t:\texttt{Nat}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}\ (x.M)\ s\ (x,y.u)\ t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}\ (x.M[q(\overrightarrow{\sigma},x)])\ (s[\overrightarrow{\sigma}])\ (x,y.u[q(q(\overrightarrow{\sigma},x),y)])\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]):M[\overrightarrow{\sigma},t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]/x]}

7.7.
Universes

Universes are where types reside. They are in a predicative hierarchy and universes
are indexed by meta-level natural numbers, so that Russell’s paradox does not
occur. In this note, we use cumulative universes, so that every type in a lower level
also resides in a higher level. In contrast, non-cumulativity ensures every term must
have a unique type. This property is not true with cumulativity. We work with
cumulativity primarily because then in the normalization algorithm we do not have to
always keep track of universe levels.

\frac{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\texttt{Ty}_{n}:\texttt{Ty}_{n+1}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i+1}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash T\approx T^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash T\approx T^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{i+1}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\texttt{Ty}_{n}[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx\texttt{Ty}_{n}:\texttt{Ty}_{n+1}}

7.8.
Modality

So far, we were still in MLTT, as we only operate in the top-level context in the
stack. The necessity modality allows us to manipulate the context stack depending on
the range of
unbox
levels.

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\square T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\vdash t:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\texttt{box}\ t:\square T}\par\frac{\boxed{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:\square T\quad\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}\quad|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|=n}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ t:T[\overrightarrow{I};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}]}

The equivalence rules are

\frac{\boxed{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\vdash t:T\quad\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}\quad|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|=n}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ (\texttt{box}\ t)\approx t[\overrightarrow{I};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}]:T[\overrightarrow{I};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}]}\par\frac{\boxed{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:\square T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx\texttt{box}\ (\texttt{unbox}_{1}\ t):\square T}

Substitution-related rules are

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\square T[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx\square(T[\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{1}\!\!{}]):\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\par\frac{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot\vdash t:T\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\texttt{box}\ t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx\texttt{box}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{1}\!\!{}]):\square T[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]}\par\frac{\boxed{\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}\quad\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash t:\square T\quad|\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}|=n\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}]\approx\texttt{unbox}_{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})}\ (t[\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n}]):T[\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})}\!\!{}]}

8.
Syntactic Properties of
Mint

In this section, we examine some syntactic properties for
Mint
. We will prove
presupposition at the end of this section to ensure we are working on a sensible type
theory. We first start with some small properties that are required to prove presupposition.

8.1.
Basic Properties

Lemma 8.1.

If
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\approx\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}
, then
|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}|=|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}|
.

Lemma 8.2.

If
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\approx\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}
and
n<|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}|
, then
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\mid{n}\approx\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\mid{n}
.

Proof.

Induction on
n
.
∎

Lemma 8.3.

If
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
and
x:T\in\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
, then
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash T
.

Proof.

We do induction on
x:T\in\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
and invert
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
.
∎

Lemma 8.4.

If
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\approx\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}
,
x:T\in\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
, then there exists
T^{\prime}
,
such that
x:T^{\prime}\in\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}
and
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash T\approx T^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We do induction on
x:T\in\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
and invert
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\approx\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}
,
so we find
x:T^{\prime}\in\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}
and apply congruence.
∎

Lemma 8.5.

If
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\approx\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}
, then
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
and
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Induction on
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\approx\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma 8.6 (Reflexivity).

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T
, then
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx t:T
.

Proof.

Induction on
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T
.
∎

Lemma 8.7 (Reflexivity).

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
, then
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
.

Proof.

Induction on
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
.
∎

Lemma 8.8 (Cumulativity).

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}
and
i\leq j
, then
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{j}
.

Proof.

Since
i\leq j
, that means
j=i+k
for some
k
. We do induction on
k
.
∎

Lemma 8.9 (Cumulativity).

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash T\approx T^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{i}
and
i\leq j
, then
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash T\approx T^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{j}
.

Proof.

Proved similarly to above.
∎

Lemma 8.10.

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash\Pi(x:S).T
, then
\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash S
and

\overrightarrow{\Gamma};(\Gamma,x:S)\vdash T
.

Proof.

Induction on
\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\Gamma\vdash\Pi(x:S).T
.
∎

Lemma 8.11.

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\square T
, then
\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\vdash T
.

Proof.

Induction on
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\square T
.
∎

We examine the properties of truncation and truncation offset:

Lemma 8.12.

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
and
n<|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|
, then
\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})<|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}|
.

Proof.

Induction on
n
and
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
.
∎

Lemma 8.13.

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
and
n<|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|
, then

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{n}
.

Proof.

Induction on
n
and
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
.
∎

Lemma 8.14.

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
and
n+m<|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|
, then

\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n+m})=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n}},{m})
.

Proof.

Induction on
n
and
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
.
∎

Lemma 8.15.

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
and
n+m<|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|
, then

\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n}\mid{m}=\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n+m}
.

Proof.

Induction on
n
and
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
.
∎

Lemma 8.16.

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
and
n<|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|
, then
\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{{}^{\prime}})n
.

Lemma 8.17.

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
and
n<|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|
, then

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{n}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}\mid{n}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\mid{n}
.

Proof.

Induction on
n
and
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
. Most cases are
immediate. We only consider two cases:

Case

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}\quad|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|=m}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\vdash(\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{m}\!\!{})\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\approx(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ(\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{m}));\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{m})}\!\!{}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\cdot}

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}({(\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{m}\!\!{})\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}},{1+n})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{m+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{m})+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{m}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ(\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{m}));\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{m})}\!\!{}},{1+n})

Moreover,

\displaystyle((\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{m}\!\!{})\circ\overrightarrow{\delta})\mid{1+n}=(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{m})\mid{n}=((\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{m});\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{m})}\!\!{})\mid{1+n}

We also know

\frac{\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\hskip 8.19447pt\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{m})}\vdash\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{m}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{m})}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{m}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{m})}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{m}},{n})}\vdash(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{m})\mid{n}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\mid{n}}

so we can apply reflexivity for the equivalence judgment. At last, we shall
examine the truncation size of
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}
is
\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{m+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})})
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{m})+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{m}},{n})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{m})+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{m}},{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{m+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{n})})

Case

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot\quad|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|>0}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{1};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{1})}\!\!{}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{1};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{1})}\!\!{}},{1+n})=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{1})+\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{1}},{n})=\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{1+n})

By the following equation, the other goal is immediate:

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{1+n}=\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{1}\mid{n}=(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\mid{1};\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\sigma}},{1})}\!\!{})\mid{1+n}

∎

8.2.
Context Stack Conversion

Lemma 8.18 (Context stack conversion).

If
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\approx\overrightarrow{\Delta}
, and

•

if
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T
, then
\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash t:T
;

•

if
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx t^{\prime}:T
, then
\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash t\approx t^{\prime}:T
;

•

if
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
, then
\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
;

•

if
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}
, then
\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We do induction on each premise derivation.

Case

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}=\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma
and
\overrightarrow{\Delta}=\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\Delta

\frac{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\quad x:T\in\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash x:T}

(by
Lemma

8.5
)

\displaystyle\vdash\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\Delta

(by
Lemma

8.4
)

\displaystyle x:T^{\prime}\in\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\Delta\text{ and }\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash T\approx T^{\prime}

(by IH)

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\Delta\vdash T\approx T^{\prime}

\frac{\frac{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\Delta\hskip 8.19447ptx:T^{\prime}\in\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\Delta}{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\Delta\vdash x:T^{\prime}}\quad\frac{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\Delta\vdash T\approx T^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\Delta\vdash T^{\prime}\approx T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}}{\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\Delta\vdash x:T}

Case

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}=\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma
and
\overrightarrow{\Delta}=\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\Delta

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash S:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};(\Gamma,x:S)\vdash t:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash\lambda x.t:\Pi(x:S).T}

(by IH)

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\Delta\vdash S:\texttt{Ty}_{i}

\displaystyle\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};(\Gamma,x:S)\approx\overrightarrow{\Delta};(\Delta,x:S)

(by IH)

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};(\Delta,x:S)\vdash t:T

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\Delta\vdash\lambda x.t:\Pi(x:S).T

Case

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\square T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}}

(by IH)

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash\square T:\texttt{Ty}_{i}

Case

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\vdash t:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\texttt{box}\ t:\square T}

\displaystyle\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\approx\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot

(by IH)

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot\vdash t:T

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash\texttt{box}\ t:\square T

Case

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}=\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}
and

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ t:T[\overrightarrow{I};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}]

Case

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}=\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma
and
\overrightarrow{\Delta}=\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\Delta

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash S:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};(\Gamma,x:S)\vdash t:T\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash s:S}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash(\lambda x.t)\ s\approx t[\overrightarrow{I},s/x]:T[\overrightarrow{I},s/x]}

\frac{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{I}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}^{\prime};\cdot

∎

As a corollary, we prove the following theorem:

Lemma 8.19.

\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\approx\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}
is a PER.

Proof.

Because
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx t^{\prime}:T
is a PER and
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\approx\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}
is
defined pointwise. Apply context stack conversion in transitivity.
∎

8.3.
Presupposition

Lemma 8.20 (presupposition).

•

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T
, then
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
and
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash T
.

•

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx t^{\prime}:T
, then
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
,
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:T
,

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t^{\prime}:T
and
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash T
.

•

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
, then
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
and
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Delta}
.

•

If
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}\approx\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
, then
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
,

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
,
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
and
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Delta}
.

Proof.

We do induction on each premise derivation.

Case

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}=\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma

\frac{\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\quad x:T\in\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash x:T}

We obtain
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash T
by
Lemma

8.3
.

Case

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}=\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash S:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};(\Gamma,x:S)\vdash t:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash\lambda x.t:\Pi(x:S).T}

(by IH)

\displaystyle\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma

(by IH)

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};(\Gamma,x:S)\vdash T

(for some
j
by
Lemma

8.8
)

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash S:\texttt{Ty}_{j}\text{ and }\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};(\Gamma,x:S)\vdash T:\texttt{Ty}_{j}

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash\Pi(x:S).T:\texttt{Ty}_{j}

Case

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:\Pi(x:S).T\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash s:S}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\ s:T[\overrightarrow{I},s/x]}

(by IH)

\displaystyle\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}

(by IH)

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\Pi(x:S).T

(by
Lemma

8.10
, where
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}=\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma
)

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};(\Gamma,x:S)\vdash T

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash T[\overrightarrow{I},s/x]

Case

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\vdash t:T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\texttt{box}\ t:\square T}

(by IH)

\displaystyle\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot

\displaystyle\vdash\Gamma

(by IH)

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\vdash T

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\square T

Case

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}=\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vdash t:\square T\quad\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\quad|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}|=n}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ t:T[\overrightarrow{I};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}]}

(by
Lemma

8.11
)

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\cdot\vdash T

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\vdash T[\overrightarrow{I};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}]

Case

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}=\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma

(by IH)

(by

\displaystyle\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\text{ and }\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash S

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash\Pi(x:S).T

Case

(by IH)

\displaystyle\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\text{ and }\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash S:\texttt{Ty}_{i}\text{ and }\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\Gamma\vdash S^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{i}

(by IH)

(by definition)

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};(\Gamma,x:S^{\prime})\vdash T^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{i}

Case

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\vdash t:T\quad\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}\quad|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|=n}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\overrightarrow{\Delta}\vdash\texttt{unbox}_{n}\ (\texttt{box}\ t)\approx t[\overrightarrow{I};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}]:T[\overrightarrow{I};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}]}

Immediate.

Case

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t:\square T}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash t\approx\texttt{box}\ (\texttt{unbox}_{1}\ t):\square T}

Notice we have

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot\vdash\overrightarrow{I}\approx\overrightarrow{I};():\overrightarrow{\Gamma};\cdot

This allows us to conclude
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\texttt{box}\ (\texttt{unbox}_{1}\ t):\square T
.

Case

\overrightarrow{\Gamma}=\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}\quad\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\quad|\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}|=n}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{n}\!\!{}:\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot}

We only need to prove
\vdash\overrightarrow{\Delta};\cdot
which we can get from

\vdash\overrightarrow{\Delta}
after applying IH to
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Delta}
.

Case

\frac{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\overrightarrow{\sigma}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}\quad\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime};\overrightarrow{\Delta}\quad|\overrightarrow{\Delta}|=m}{\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\vdash(\overrightarrow{\sigma};\Uparrow^{m}\!\!{})\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\approx(\overrightarrow{\sigma}\circ\overrightarrow{\delta}\mid{m});\Uparrow^{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{m})}\!\!{}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime};\cdot}

(by IH)

\displaystyle\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime}

(by IH)

\displaystyle\vdash\overrightarrow{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Gamma}\mid{\mathcal{O}({\overrightarrow{\delta}},{m})}\vdash\overrightarrow{\delta}:\overrightarrow{\Gamma}^{\prime\prime}

We can derive the goal based on these judgments and the premises.

∎

8.4.
Removing Boxed Premises

With presupposition, we are able to remove those boxed premises. The systems with and
without those premises can be proved equivalent.

Theorem 8.21.

We can remove the boxed premises and the resulting system remains equivalent to the
one before the removal.

We do this by showing equivalence of typing and equivalence judgments. We apply
presupposition whenever necessary. This theorem also implies the resulting system also
has presupposition.
